(although the corollary is stated for
V
open, the proof works obviously when
V
is merely measurable).

Proposition 7.4
.

(
Taylor expansion of the mass, see
[
12
, Corollary 3.3]
)
. There are dimensional constants
c,C>0
such that the following holds. Let
V\subset\mathbb{R}^{2}
be a bounded measurable set and let
u:V\rightarrow\mathcal{A}_{Q}\left(\mathbb{R}^{n}\right)
be a Lipschitz function with
\operatorname{Lip}(u)\leq c.
Denote by
\mathbf{G}_{u}
the integer rectifiable current associated to the graph of
u
as in
[
12
, Definition 1.10]
. Then, the following Taylor expansion of the mass of
\mathbf{G}_{u}
holds:

\mathbf{M}\left(\mathbf{G}_{u}\right)=Q|V|+\int_{V}\frac{|Du|^{2}}{2}+\int_{V}\sum_{i}R\left(Du_{i}\right),

where
R:\mathbb{R}^{n\times 2}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
is a
C^{1}
function satisfying
|R(D)|=|D|^{3}L(D)
for some positive function
L
such that
L(0)=0
and
\operatorname{Lip}(L)\leq C
.

Remark 7.5
.

We write here the analog of (
[
11
, Remark 5.5]
). There exists a dimensional constant
c>0
such that, if
E\leq c,
then the
E^{\beta}
-Lipschitz approximation satisfies the following estimates:

\operatorname{Lip}(f)\leq C(E+C\mathbf{A}^{2})^{\beta},

(7.4)

\int_{B_{3s}(x)\cap D}|Df|^{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:stimaK}}}{{\leq}}C(E+\mathbf{A}^{2})s^{2}.

(7.5)

Indeed (
7.4
) follows from Proposition
6.4
, by the choice of
\beta
and the scaling of
\mathbf{A}.
While (
7.5
) follows from Proposition
7.4
since for
E
sufficiently small

\int_{B_{3s}(x)\cap D}\sum_{i}R\left(Df_{i}\right)\leq CE^{2\beta}\int_{B_{3s}(x)\cap D}|Df|^{2}<\frac{1}{4}\int_{B_{3s}(x)\cap D}|Df|^{2},

and therefore

\displaystyle\int_{B_{3s}(x)\cap D}|Df|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\mathbf{M}\left(\mathbf{G}_{f}\mathop{\hbox{\vrule height=7.0pt,width=0.3pt,depth=0.0pt\vrule height=0.3pt,width=5.0pt,depth=0.0pt}}\nolimits\mathbf{C}_{3s}(x)\cap(D\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\right)-Q|D|\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\mathbf{M}\left(T\mathop{\hbox{\vrule height=7.0pt,width=0.3pt,depth=0.0pt\vrule height=0.3pt,width=5.0pt,depth=0.0pt}}\nolimits\mathbf{C}_{3s}(x)\right)-Q|D|\right)+C\mathbf{M}\left(\mathbf{G}_{f}\mathop{\hbox{\vrule height=7.0pt,width=0.3pt,depth=0.0pt\vrule height=0.3pt,width=5.0pt,depth=0.0pt}}\nolimits\left(B_{3s}(x)\cap D\setminus K\right)\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\right)

\displaystyle\leq CEs^{2}+C(E+\mathbf{A}^{2})^{2\beta}\left|B_{3s}(x)\cap D\setminus K\right|\leq C(E+\mathbf{A}^{2})s^{2}.

Proof of Theorem
7.3
.

By rescaling, it is not restrictive to assume that
r=1
. The proof of (
7.1
) is by contradiction. Assume there exist a constant
c_{1}>0,
a sequence of currents
\left(T_{k}\right)_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
satisfying Assumption
6.2
and corresponding
E_{k}^{\beta}
-Lipschitz approximations
\left(f_{k}\right)_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
which violate (
7.1
) for
\eta=c_{1}>0
.
At the same time
\partial T\mathop{\hbox{\vrule height=7.0pt,width=0.3pt,depth=0.0pt\vrule height=0.3pt,width=5.0pt,depth=0.0pt}}\nolimits{\mathbf{C}}_{4}(0)=Q\left\llbracket{\Gamma_{k}}\right\rrbracket
, where
\Gamma_{k}
is a sequence of
C^{2}
curves. For the latter we have
T_{0}\Gamma_{k}=\mathbb{R}\times\{0\}
and a parametrization
\psi^{k}:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
of the form

\psi^{k}(t)=(\psi^{k}_{1}(t),\bar{\psi}^{k}(t))\,.

Moreover we assume
\|\psi^{k}\|_{C^{2}}\leq C\mathbf{A}_{k}\leq C\varepsilon_{k}E_{k}^{\frac{1}{2}}
.
The domain of definition of the map
f_{k}
is a set
D_{k}
which can be explicitly written as

D_{k}=\{(x_{1},x_{2})\in B_{3}:x_{2}>\psi_{1}^{k}(x_{1})\}\,.

Summarizing, our currents satisfy the following:

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left(T_{k},\mathbf{C}_{4}\right)\leq\varepsilon_{k}\rightarrow 0,\quad\mathbf{A}_{k}\leq\varepsilon_{k}E^{\frac{1}{2}}_{k}\quad\text{ and }\quad\int_{D_{k}\setminus K_{k}}\left|Df_{k}\right|^{2}\geq c_{1}E_{k}

(7.6)

where
K_{k}:=\left\{x\in B_{3}:\mathbf{me}_{T_{k}}(x)<E_{k}^{2\beta}\right\}
.
Set
\Lambda_{k}:=\left\{x\in D_{k}:\mathbf{me}_{T_{k}}(x)\leq 2^{-2}E_{k}^{2\beta}\right\}

and observe that
\Lambda_{k}\cap B_{3}\subset K_{k}
. From Proposition
6.4
it follows that

\displaystyle\operatorname{Lip}\left(f_{k}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle CE_{k}^{\beta}

(7.7)

\displaystyle\left|B_{r}\cap D_{k}\setminus K_{k}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle CE_{k}^{-2\beta}\mathbf{e}_{T_{k}}\left(B_{r+r_{0}(k)}\setminus\Lambda_{k}\right)+C\varepsilon_{k}^{2}E_{k}^{2(1-\beta)}\quad\text{ for every }r\leq 3

(7.8)

where
r_{0}(k)=16E_{k}^{(1-2\beta)/2}<\frac{1}{2}
. Then, (
7.6
), (
7.7
), and (
7.8
) give

c_{1}E_{k}\leq\int_{B_{2}\cap D_{k}\setminus K_{k}}\left|Df_{k}\right|^{2}\leq C\mathbf{e}_{T_{k}}\left(B_{s}\setminus\Lambda_{k}\right)+C\varepsilon_{k}^{2}E_{k}^{2}\quad\text{ for every }s\in\left[\frac{5}{2},3\right].

(7.9)

Setting
c_{2}:=c_{1}/(2C),
we have

2c_{2}E_{k}\leq\mathbf{e}_{T_{k}}\left(B_{s}\cap D_{k}\setminus\Lambda_{k}\right)=\mathbf{e}_{T_{k}}\left(B_{s}\cap D_{k}\right)-\mathbf{e}_{T_{k}}\left(B_{s}\cap\Lambda_{k}\right)\,,

implying

\mathbf{e}_{T_{k}}\left(\Lambda_{k}\cap B_{s}\right)\leq\mathbf{e}_{T_{k}}\left(D_{k}\cap B_{s}\right)-2c_{2}E_{k}\,.

(7.10)

Next observe that
2\pi 4^{2}E_{k}=\mathbf{e}_{T_{k}}\left(B_{4}\cap D_{k}\right)\geq\mathbf{e}_{T_{k}}\left(B_{s}\cap D_{k}\right)
. Therefore, by the Taylor expansion in
[
11
, Remark 5.4]
, (
7.10
) and the fact that
E_{k}\downarrow 0
, it follows that for every
s\in[5/2,3]
and
k
large enough so that
CE^{2\beta_{k}}\leq c_{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\Lambda_{k}\cap B_{s}}\frac{\left|Df_{k}\right|^{2}}{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{Taylor}}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle\left(1+CE_{k}^{2\beta}\right)\mathbf{e}_{T_{k}}\left(\Lambda_{k}\cap B_{s}\right)

(7.11)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:5.10}}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle\left(1+CE_{k}^{2\beta}\right)\left(\mathbf{e}_{T_{k}}\left(B_{s}\cap D_{k}\right)-2c_{2}E_{k}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbf{e}_{T_{k}}\left(B_{s}\cap D_{k}\right)-c_{2}E_{k}\,.

Our aim is to show that (
7.11
) contradicts the minimality of
T_{k}
. To construct a competitor, we write
f_{k}(x)=\sum_{i}\llbracket f_{k}^{i}(x)\rrbracket\in\mathcal{A}_{Q}\left(\mathbb{R}^{n}\right)
. We consider
h_{k}:=E_{k}^{-1/2}f_{k}
. Observe that
h_{k}|_{\partial D_{k}}=Q\llbracket E_{k}^{-1/2}\bar{\psi}^{k}\rrbracket
and that in turn
\|\bar{\psi}^{k}\|_{C^{2}}\leq C\varepsilon_{k}E_{k}^{\frac{1}{2}}
. In particular
E_{k}^{-1/2}\bar{\psi}^{k}
converges strongly to
0
in
C^{2}
. Extend
\bar{\psi}^{k}
to
B_{3}\cap D_{k}
by keeping it constant in the variable
x_{2}
. Thus
{\mathcal{G}}(h_{k},Q\llbracket E_{k}^{-1/2}\bar{\psi}^{k}\rrbracket)
is a classical
W^{1,2}
function that vanishes on
\partial D_{k}
. Since by
[
11
, Remark 5.5(5.5)]
we have
\sup_{k}\textup{Dir}\left(h_{k},B_{3}\cap D\right)<\infty
, the Poincaré inequality gives

\|{\mathcal{G}}(h_{k},Q\llbracket E_{k}^{-1/2}\bar{\psi}^{k}\rrbracket)\|_{L^{2}(D_{k}\cap B_{3})}\leq C\,,

which in turn implies
\|{\mathcal{G}}(h_{k},Q\left\llbracket{0}\right\rrbracket)\|_{L^{2}(D_{k}\cap B_{3})}\leq C
. Hence
\{h_{k}\}
is bounded in
W^{1,2}
. Even though the domains of the
h_{k}
depend on
k
, we can extend the maps identically equal to
Q\{\bar{\psi}^{k}\}
on their complement, and thus treat them as maps on
B_{3}
. Up to a subsequence, not relabeled, we can thus assume that the maps converge to some
h\in W^{1,2}
. Observe that
h
vanishes identically on the lower half disk
B_{3}^{-}:=\{(x_{1},x_{2})\in B_{3}:x_{2}<0\}
and thus we will also consider it as a map defined on the upper half disk
B_{3}^{+}
, taking the value
Q\left\llbracket{0}\right\rrbracket
on the
x_{1}
-axis.

Since

\left\|\mathcal{G}\left(h_{k},h\right)\right\|_{L^{2}\left(B_{3}\right)}\rightarrow 0

(7.12)

and the following inequalities hold for every
open
\Omega^{\prime}\subset B_{3}
and any sequence of measurable sets
J_{k}
with
\left|J_{k}\right|\rightarrow 0
,

\displaystyle\liminf_{k\rightarrow+\infty}\left(\int_{\Omega^{\prime}\setminus J_{k}}\left|Dh_{k}\right|^{2}-\int_{\Omega^{\prime}}\left|Dh\right|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle 0,

(7.13)

\displaystyle\limsup_{k\rightarrow+\infty}\int_{\Omega}\left(\left|Dh_{k}\right|-\left|Dh\right|\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\limsup_{k\to+\infty}\int_{\Omega}\left(\left|Dh_{k}\right|^{2}-\left|Dh\right|^{2}\right).

(7.14)

Applying the first inequality with
J_{k}
being the complement of
\Lambda_{k}
we reach the following inequality

\frac{1}{2}\int_{B_{s}^{+}}|Dh|^{2}\leq\liminf_{k\to\infty}E_{k}^{-1}{\mathbf{e}}_{T_{k}}(B_{s}\cap D_{k})-c_{2}\qquad\mbox{for every $s<3$.}

(7.15)

Now we wish to find a radius
r\in[\frac{5}{2},3]
and a competitor function
H_{k}
such that

•

\left.H_{k}\right|_{(B_{3}\setminus B_{r})\cap D_{k}}=\left.h_{k}\right|_{(B_{3}\setminus B_{r})\cap D_{k}}
;

•

\left.H_{k}\right|_{\partial D_{k}\cap B_{3}}=\left.h_{k}\right|_{\partial D_{k}\cap B_{3}}
;

•

The following estimates hold for a subsequence (not relabeled)

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\operatorname{Dir}\left(H_{k},B_{r}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\operatorname{Dir}\left(h,B_{r}\right)+\frac{c_{2}}{4},

(7.16)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Lip}\left(H_{k}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C^{*}E_{k}^{\beta-1/2}\,,

(7.17)

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{G}}(H_{k},h_{k})\|_{L^{2}(B^{+}_{r})}

\displaystyle\leq C\textup{Dir}(h_{k},B^{+}_{r})+C\textup{Dir}(H_{k},B^{+}_{r})\leq M<+\infty,

(7.18)

where
C^{*}
is a constant independent of
k
.

After proving that such a function exists, we can then follow the proof of
[
11
, Theorem 5.2]
mutatis mutandis.

In order to show our claim we will use (
7.12
), the Lipschitz bound
{\rm{Lip}}(h_{k})\leq CE_{k}^{\beta-1/2}
, the bound
\sup_{k}\textup{Dir}(h_{k},B_{3})\leq C
, and (
7.15
). Note next that, since
\|\bar{\psi}^{k}/E^{1/2}\|_{C^{2}}\downarrow 0
, all these facts remain true if we replace
h_{k}
with the map

\bar{h}_{k}(x):=\sum_{i}\left\llbracket{(h_{k})_{i}-\bar{\psi}^{k}}\right\rrbracket\,.

The advantage of the latter is that
\bar{h}_{k}|_{\partial D_{k}}=Q\left\llbracket{0}\right\rrbracket
. Assuming that we find corresponding maps
\bar{H}_{k}
satisfying all the properties above, we can then simply get
H_{k}
by adding back
\bar{\psi}^{k}
:

H_{k}(x)=\sum_{i}\left\llbracket{(\bar{H}_{k})_{i}+\bar{\psi}^{k}}\right\rrbracket\,

(because the difference in the Dirichlet energies of
H_{k}
and
\bar{H}_{k}
and the difference in the Lipschitz constants are both infinitesimal).

The next issue is that the domains
D_{k}\cap B_{s}
are curved compared to
B_{s}^{+}
. To resolve this, we invoke Lemma
7.6
below. For each
k
we apply the lemma to
\psi^{k}_{1}
and get a corresponding diffeomorphism
\Phi_{k}
which maps each
B_{s}\cap D_{k}
diffeomorphically onto
B_{s}^{+}
. Observe that

\lim_{k\to\infty}\left(\|\Phi_{k}-{\rm Id}\|_{C^{1}}+\|\Phi_{k}^{-1}-{\rm Id}\|_{C^{1}}\right)=0

(7.19)

because
\|\psi^{k}_{1}\|_{C^{1}}\to 0
. For this reason the maps
\tilde{h}_{k}:=\bar{h}_{k}\circ\Phi_{k}^{-1}
satisfy the same assumptions as
\bar{h}_{k}
(and hence as
h_{k}
). Indeed, after having built the corresponding competitors
\tilde{H}_{k}
, we can then define
\bar{H}_{k}:=\tilde{H}_{k}\circ\Phi_{k}
. Again the desired conclusion follows
because the difference of the Lipschitz constants and Dirichlet energies are infinitesimal.

Summarizing, we have reduced the proof of the proposition to showing that the competitor
H_{k}
can be constructed, without loss of generality, under the additional assumptions that all
h_{k}
’s are defined on the same domain
B_{3}^{+}
and that they all vanish on
\{(x_{1},x_{2})\in B_{3}^{+}:x_{2}=0\}
. This is accomplished in Proposition
7.7
below. Now that we have illustrated how to construct suitable competitors we can proceed with the proof of the theorem. We restart observing that, when
k
is large enough, (
7.13
) implies the following inequalities

\operatorname{Dir}\left(h,B_{r}\right)\leq\operatorname{Dir}\left(h_{k},B_{r}\cap\Gamma_{k}\right)+\frac{c_{2}}{4}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(5.11)}}{{\leq}}\frac{\mathbf{e}_{T_{k}}\left(B_{r}\right)}{E_{k}}-\frac{3c_{2}}{4}E_{k}\,.

(7.20)

Note that (
7.17
) follows from (
7.27
) as
E_{k}^{\beta-1/2}\uparrow\infty
. Thus
C^{*}
depends on
c_{2}
and on the choice of the two sequences, but not on
k
. From now on, although this and similar constants are not dimensional, we will keep denoting them by
C,
with the understanding that they do not depend on
k
. Note that, from (
7.7
) and (
7.8
), one gets

\displaystyle\left\|T_{k}-\mathbf{G}_{f_{k}}\right\|\left(\mathbf{C}_{3}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\left\|T_{k}\right\|\left(\left(B_{3}\setminus K_{k}\right)\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\right)+\left\|\mathbf{G}_{f_{k}}\right\|\left(\left(B_{3}\setminus K_{k}\right)\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\right)

\displaystyle\leq Q\left|B_{3}\setminus K_{k}\right|+E_{k}+Q\left|B_{3}\setminus K_{k}\right|+C\left|B_{3}\setminus K_{k}\right|\operatorname{Lip}\left(f_{k}\right)

\displaystyle\leq E_{k}+CE_{k}^{1-2\beta}\leq CE_{k}^{1-2\beta}.

Let
(z,y)
denote the coordinates on
\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
and consider the function
\varphi(z,y)=|z|
and the slice
\left\langle T_{k}-\mathbf{G}_{f_{k}},\varphi,r\right\rangle.
Observe that, by the coarea formula and Fatou’s lemma,

\int_{r}^{3}\liminf_{k}E_{k}^{2\beta-1}\mathbf{M}\left(\left\langle T_{k}-\mathbf{G}_{f_{k}},\varphi,s\right\rangle\right)ds\leq\liminf_{k}E_{k}^{2\beta-1}\left\|T_{k}-\mathbf{G}_{f_{k}}\right\|\left(\mathbf{C}_{3}\right)\leq C.

Therefore, for some
\bar{r}\in(r,3)
, up to subsequences (not relabeled)
\mathbf{M}\left(\left\langle T_{k}-\mathbf{G}_{f_{k}},\varphi,\bar{r}\right\rangle\right)

\leq CE_{k}^{1-2\beta}
.
Let now
v_{k}:=E_{k}^{1/2}H_{k}|_{B_{\bar{r}}}
and consider the current
Z_{k}:=\mathbf{G}_{v_{k}}\mathop{\hbox{\vrule height=7.0pt,width=0.3pt,depth=0.0pt\vrule height=0.3pt,width=5.0pt,depth=0.0pt}}\nolimits\mathbf{C}_{\bar{r}}
. Since
(v_{k})|_{\partial B_{\bar{r}}}=\left.f_{k}\right|_{\partial B_{\bar{r}}},
one gets
\partial Z_{k}=\left\langle\mathbf{G}_{f_{k}},\varphi,\bar{r}\right\rangle
and hence,
\mathbf{M}\left(\partial\left(T_{k}\mathop{\hbox{\vrule height=7.0pt,width=0.3pt,depth=0.0pt\vrule height=0.3pt,width=5.0pt,depth=0.0pt}}\nolimits\mathbf{C}_{\bar{r}}\right.\right.

\left.\left.-Z_{k}\right)\right)\leq CE_{k}^{1-2\beta}.
We define

S_{k}=T_{k}\mathop{\hbox{\vrule height=7.0pt,width=0.3pt,depth=0.0pt\vrule height=0.3pt,width=5.0pt,depth=0.0pt}}\nolimits\left(\mathbf{C}_{4}\setminus\mathbf{C}_{\bar{r}}\right)+Z_{k}+R_{k}\,,

(7.21)

where (cp.
[
11
, Remark 5.3]
)
R_{k}
is an integral current such that

\partial R_{k}=\partial\left(T_{k}\mathop{\hbox{\vrule height=7.0pt,width=0.3pt,depth=0.0pt\vrule height=0.3pt,width=5.0pt,depth=0.0pt}}\nolimits\mathbf{C}_{\bar{r}}-Z_{k}\right)\quad\text{ and }\quad\mathbf{M}\left(R_{k}\right)\leq CE_{k}^{(1-2\beta)2}\,.

In particular, we have
\partial S_{k}=\partial\left(T_{k}\mathop{\hbox{\vrule height=7.0pt,width=0.3pt,depth=0.0pt\vrule height=0.3pt,width=5.0pt,depth=0.0pt}}\nolimits\mathbf{C}_{4}\right)
. We now show that, since
\beta<\frac{1}{4},
for
k
large enough, the mass of
S_{k}
is strictly smaller than the one of
T_{k}.
To this aim we write

\displaystyle\operatorname{Dir}\left(v_{k},B_{\bar{r}}\right)-\operatorname{Dir}\left(f_{k},B_{\bar{r}}\cap\Lambda_{k}\right)=

\displaystyle\int_{B_{\bar{r}}}\left|Dv_{k}\right|^{2}-\int_{B_{\bar{r}}\cap\Lambda_{k}}\left|Df_{k}\right|^{2}=:I_{1}\,.

The first term is estimated by (
7.16
) and (
7.13
). Indeed, recall that
v_{k}=E_{k}^{1/2}H_{k}
and
f_{k}=E_{k}^{1/2}h_{k}
(but also that the two functions coincide on
B_{\bar{r}}\setminus B_{r}
). We thus deduce that
I_{1}\leq\frac{c_{2}}{2}E_{k}
for
k
large enough. Hence,

\displaystyle\mathbf{M}\left(S_{k}\right)-\mathbf{M}\left(T_{k}\right)

\displaystyle\ \leq\mathbf{M}\left(Z_{k}\right)+C\mathbf{M}\left(R_{k}\right)-\mathbf{M}\left(T_{k}\mathop{\hbox{\vrule height=7.0pt,width=0.3pt,depth=0.0pt\vrule height=0.3pt,width=5.0pt,depth=0.0pt}}\nolimits\mathbf{C}_{\bar{r}}\right)

\displaystyle\ \leq Q\left|B_{\bar{r}}\right|+\int_{B_{\bar{r}}}\frac{\left|Dv_{k}\right|^{2}}{2}+CE_{k}^{1+2\beta}+CE_{k}^{(1-2\beta)2}-Q\left|B_{\bar{r}}\right|-\mathbf{e}_{T_{k}}\left(B_{\bar{r}}\right)

\displaystyle\ \leq\int_{B_{\bar{r}}\cap\Lambda_{k}}\frac{\left|Df_{k}\right|^{2}}{2}+\frac{1}{2}c_{2}E_{k}+CE_{k}^{1+2\beta}+CE_{k}^{(1-2\beta)2}-\mathbf{e}_{T_{k}}\left(B_{\bar{r}}\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:5.11DS3}}}{{\leq}}-\frac{c_{2}E_{k}}{2}+CE_{k}^{1+\beta}+CE_{k}^{(1-2\beta)2}<0,

(7.22)

as soon as
E_{k}
is small enough, i.e.,
k
large enough. This gives the desired contradiction and proves (
7.1
).

Now, we come to the proof of (
7.2
) and (
7.3
). To this aim, we argue again by contradiction using similar constructions of competitors. Without loss of generality, we assume
x=0
and
s=1
. Suppose
\left(T_{k}\right)_{k}
is a sequence with
E_{k}:=\mathbf{E}\left(T_{k},\mathbf{C}_{4}\right)
satisfying

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left(T_{k},\mathbf{C}_{4}\right)\leq\varepsilon_{k}\rightarrow 0,\qquad\mathbf{A}_{k}\leq\varepsilon_{k}E^{\frac{1}{2}}_{k},

(7.23)

but contradicting (
7.2
) or (
7.3
). Let us denote by
f_{k}
the
E_{k}^{\beta}
-Lipschitz approximation of
T_{k}
. We know that, for any sequence of Dir-minimizing functions
\bar{u}_{k}
which we might choose, we will have by the contradiction assumption that

\liminf_{k}\underbrace{E_{k}^{-1}\int_{B_{2}}\left(\mathcal{G}\left(f_{k},\bar{u}_{k}\right)^{2}+\left(\left|Df_{k}\right|-\left|D\bar{u}_{k}\right|\right)^{2}+\left|D\left(\bm{\eta}\circ f_{k}-\bm{\eta}\circ\bar{u}_{k}\right)\right|^{2}\right)}_{=:I(k)}>0.

(7.24)

As in the previous argument, we introduce the auxiliary normalized functions
h_{k}=E_{k}^{-1/2}f_{k}
and, after extraction of a subsequence, the function
h
satisfies (
7.13
) and (
7.14
). Moreover
\left\|\mathcal{G}\left(h_{k},h\right)\right\|_{L^{2}\left(B_{3}\right)}\rightarrow 0.
We next claim (and prove)

(i)

\lim_{k}\int_{B_{2}}\left|Dh_{k}\right|^{2}=\int_{B_{2}}\left|Dh\right|^{2}
,

(ii)

h
is Dir-minimizing in
B_{2}
.

Indeed, if
(i)
were false, then there is a positive constant
c_{2}
such that, for any
r\in[5/2,3]
,

\int_{B_{r}}\frac{\left|Dh\right|^{2}}{2}\leq\int_{B_{r}}\frac{\left|Dh_{k}\right|^{2}}{2}-c_{2}\leq\frac{\mathbf{e}_{T_{k}}\left(B_{r}\right)}{E_{k}}-\frac{c_{2}}{2},

(7.25)

provided
k
is large enough (where the last inequality is again an effect of the Taylor expansion of
[
11
, Remark 5.4]
). We next define the competitor currents
S_{k}
as in the argument leading to (
7.22
). Replacing in the argument above (
7.11
) and (
7.20
) by (
7.25
), we deduce again (
7.22
). On the other hand (
7.22
) contradicts the minimality of
T_{k}
. So we conclude that
(i)
is true.

If
(ii)
were false, then
h
is not Dir-minimizing in
B_{2}
. Thus, we can find a competitor
\tilde{h}\in W^{1,2}(B_{3},{\mathcal{A}}_{Q}({\mathbb{R}}^{n}))
with less energy in the ball
B_{2}
than
h
and such that
\tilde{h}=h
on
B_{3}\setminus B_{5/2}
. So for any
r\in[5/2,3]
, the function
\tilde{h}
satisfies

\int_{B_{r}}\frac{\big|D\tilde{h}\big|^{2}}{2}\leq\int_{B_{r}}\frac{\left|Dh\right|^{2}}{2}-c_{2}=\lim_{k\to\infty}\int_{B_{r}}\frac{\left|Dh_{k}\right|^{2}}{2}-c_{2}\leq\frac{\mathbf{e}_{T}\left(B_{r}\right)}{E_{k}}-\frac{c_{2}}{2},

(7.26)

provided
k
is large enough (here
c_{2}>0
is some constant independent of
r
and
k
). On the other hand,
\tilde{h}=h
on
B_{3}\setminus B_{5/2}
and therefore
\left\|\mathcal{G}\left(\tilde{h},h_{k}\right)\right\|_{L^{2}\left(B_{3}\setminus B_{5/2}\right)}\rightarrow 0
.
We then construct the competitor current
S_{k}
of (
7.21
). This time however, we use the map
\tilde{h}
in place of
h
to construct
H_{k}
via Proposition
7.7
and we reach the contradiction (
7.22
) using (
7.26
) in place of (
7.11
) and (
7.20
). We next set
\bar{u}_{k}:=E_{k}^{1/2}h
and we will show that
I(k)\rightarrow 0
, violating (
7.24
). Observe first that as
\left\|\mathcal{G}\left(h_{k},h\right)\right\|_{L^{2}}\rightarrow 0,
we have
D\left(\bm{\xi}\circ h_{k}\right)-D\left(\bm{\xi}\circ h\right)\rightarrow 0
weakly in
L^{2}
(recall the definition of
\bm{\xi}=\bm{\xi}_{BW}
in
[
11
, Section 2.5]
). So, (i) and the identities
\left|D\left(\bm{\xi}\circ h_{k}\right)\right|=\left|Dh_{k}\right|
,
\left|D\left(\bm{\xi}\circ h\right)\right|=\left|Dh\right|
imply that
D\left(\bm{\xi}\circ h_{k}\right)-D\left(\bm{\xi}\circ h\right)
converges strongly to
0
in
L^{2}
.
If we next set
\hat{h}=\sum_{i}\llbracket h^{i}-\bm{\eta}\circ h\rrbracket
and
\hat{h}_{k}=\sum_{i}\llbracket h_{k}^{i}-\bm{\eta}\circ h_{k}\rrbracket,
we obviously have

\left\|\mathcal{G}\left(\hat{h},\hat{h}_{k}\right)\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\bm{\eta}\circ h-\bm{\eta}\circ h_{k}\right\|_{L^{2}}\rightarrow 0.
Recall however that the Dirichlet energy
enjoys the splitting

\operatorname{Dir}\left(h_{k}\right)=Q\int\left|D\left(\bm{\eta}\circ h_{k}\right)\right|^{2}+\operatorname{Dir}\left(\hat{h}_{k}\right),\quad\operatorname{Dir}\left(h\right)=Q\int\left|D\left(\bm{\eta}\circ h\right)\right|^{2}+\operatorname{Dir}\left(\hat{h}\right).

So (i) implies that the Dirichlet energy of
\bm{\eta}\circ h_{k}
and
\hat{h}_{k}
converge, respectively, to the one of
\eta\circ h
and
\hat{h}
(which, we recall again, are independent of
k
because the
h_{k}
’s are translating sheets). We thus infer that
D\left(\bm{\eta}\circ h\right)-D\left(\bm{\eta}\circ h_{k}\right)
converges to 0 strongly in
L^{2}
. Coming back to
\bar{u}_{k}
we observe that
\bar{u}_{k}
is Dir-minimizing and

E_{k}^{-1}\int_{B_{2}}\mathcal{G}\left(\bar{u}_{k},f_{k}\right)^{2}=\int_{B_{2}}\mathcal{G}\left(h,h_{k}\right)^{2}\rightarrow 0.

So,

\displaystyle\limsup_{k}I(k)\leq

\displaystyle\limsup_{k}\int_{B_{2}}\left(\left|Dh_{k}\right|-\left|Dh\right|\right)^{2}+\left|D\left(\bm{\eta}\circ h_{k}-{\bm{\eta}}\circ h\right)\right|^{2}.

Thus
I(k)\rightarrow 0,
which contradicts (
7.24
).
∎

7.1.
Technical lemmas

Lemma 7.6
.

There is a positive geometric constant
c>0
with the following property. Consider a
C^{1}
function
\psi_{1}:[0,4]\to\mathbb{R}
such that
\psi_{1}(0)=\psi_{1}^{\prime}(0)=0
and
\|\psi_{1}\|_{C^{1}}\leq c
. Then there is a map
\Phi:B_{4}\to B_{4}
such that

•

\Phi
maps
B_{s}
diffeomorphically onto itself for every
s\in(0,4]
;

•

if we set
D:=\{(x_{1},x_{2}):|x_{1}|\leq 4,x_{2}>\psi_{1}(x_{1})\}
then
\Phi
maps
D\cap B_{s}
diffeomorphically onto
B_{s}^{+}
for every
s\in(0,4]
;

•

\|\Phi^{-1}-{\rm Id}\|_{C^{1}}+\|\Phi-{\rm Id}\|_{C^{1}}\leq C\|\psi_{1}\|_{C^{1}}
.

Proof.

We use polar coordinates
(\theta,r)
and let the angle
\theta
vary from
-\frac{\pi}{2}
(included) to
\frac{3\pi}{2}
(excluded). It is in fact easier to define the map
\Phi^{-1}
.
If
c
is sufficiently small, each circle
\partial B_{s}
intersects the graph of
\psi_{1}
in exactly two points, given in polar coordinates by
(\theta_{r}(s),s)
and
(\theta_{l}(s),s)
, with
\theta_{l}(s)>\theta_{r}(s)
. Furthermore, again assuming
c
is sufficiently small,
|\theta_{r}(s)|\leq\frac{\pi}{4}
and
|\theta_{l}(s)-\pi|\leq\frac{\pi}{4}
. In polar coordinates the map
\Phi^{-1}
is then defined on
B^{+}_{4}
by the formula

\Phi^{-1}(\theta,s)=\left(\frac{\theta_{r}(s)(\pi-\theta)+\theta_{l}(s)\theta}{\pi},s\right)\,.

The verification that
\|\Phi^{-1}-{\rm Id}\|_{C^{1}}\leq C\|\psi_{1}\|_{C^{1}}
is left to the reader.

We then need to extend the map to the lower half disk keeping the same estimate. This could be reached for instance by the formula

\Phi^{-1}(\theta,s)=\left(\frac{2\pi-(\theta_{l}-\theta_{r})}{\pi}\theta e^{a(\theta-\pi)(\theta-2\pi)}+2\theta_{l}-\theta_{r},s\right)\qquad\mbox{for $\pi<\theta<2\pi$,}

where
a=a(s):=\pi^{-2}(1-\frac{\theta_{l}(s)-\theta_{r}(s)}{2\pi-(\theta_{l}(s)-\theta_{r}(s))})
.
∎

In the next proposition we want to “patch” functions defined on the upper half disk
B_{s}^{+}
which vanish on the
x_{1}
-axis. For convenience we introduce the notation
{\mathcal{H}}_{s}
horizontal boundary for
{\mathcal{H}}_{s}=\{(x_{1},0):|x_{1}|<s\}
.

Proposition 7.7
.

Consider two radii
1\leq r_{0}<r_{1}<4
and maps

h_{k},h\in W^{1,2}(B^{+}_{r_{1}},{\mathcal{A}}_{Q}({\mathbb{R}}^{n}))
satisfying

\sup_{k}\textup{Dir}(h_{k},B^{+}_{r_{1}})<+\infty\quad\text{and}\quad\|{\mathcal{G}}(h_{k},h)\|_{L^{2}(B^{+}_{r_{1}}\setminus B_{r_{0}})}\to 0

and
h_{k}|_{{\mathcal{H}}_{r_{1}}}=h|_{{\mathcal{H}}_{r_{1}}}=Q\left\llbracket{0}\right\rrbracket
.
Then for every
\eta>0
, there exist
r\in]r_{0},r_{1}[
, a subsequence of
\{h_{k}\}_{k}

(not relabeled)
and functions
H_{k}\in W^{1,2}(B^{+}_{r_{1}},{\mathcal{A}}_{Q}({\mathbb{R}}^{n}))
such that:

•

H_{k}|_{B^{+}_{r_{1}}\setminus B^{+}_{r}}=h_{k}|_{B^{+}_{r_{1}}\setminus B^{+}_{r}}
;

•

H_{k}|_{{\mathcal{H}}_{s}}=Q\left\llbracket{0}\right\rrbracket
and

•

\textup{Dir}(H_{k},B^{+}_{r_{1}})\leq\textup{Dir}(h,B^{+}_{r_{1}})+\eta
.

Moreover, there is a dimensional constant
C
and a constant
C^{*}
(depending on
\eta
and the
two sequences, but not on
k
) such that

\displaystyle{\rm{Lip}}(H_{k})

\displaystyle\leq C^{*}\,({\rm{Lip}}(h_{k})+1)\,,

(7.27)

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{G}}(H_{k},h_{k})\|_{L^{2}(B^{+}_{r})}

\displaystyle\leq C\textup{Dir}(h_{k},B^{+}_{r})+C\textup{Dir}(H_{k},B^{+}_{r})\,,

(7.28)

\displaystyle\|{\bm{\eta}}\circ H_{k}\|_{L^{1}(B^{+}_{r_{1}})}

\displaystyle\leq C^{*}\,\|{\bm{\eta}}\circ h_{k}\|_{L^{1}(B^{+}_{r_{1}})}+C\|{\bm{\eta}}\circ h\|_{L^{1}(B^{+}_{r_{1}})}\,.

(7.29)

Before coming to the proof of the proposition we state the following variant of the Lipschitz approximation in
[
11
, Lemma 4.5]
. Observe that the only difference is that our functions are defined on the upper half disks and vanish on the horizontal boundary. We need the Lipschitz approximation
f_{\varepsilon}
to satisfy the same requirement.

Lemma 7.8

(Lusin type Lipschitz approximation)
.

Let
f\in W^{1,2}(B^{+}_{r},{\mathcal{A}}_{Q})
be such that
f|_{{\mathcal{H}}_{r}}=Q\left\llbracket{0}\right\rrbracket
.
Then for every
\varepsilon>0
there exists
f_{\varepsilon}\in{\rm{Lip}}(B^{+}_{r},{\mathcal{A}}_{Q})
satisfying
f_{\varepsilon}|_{{\mathcal{H}}_{r}}=Q\left\llbracket{0}\right\rrbracket
and

\displaystyle\int_{B^{+}_{r}}{\mathcal{G}}(f,f_{\varepsilon})^{2}+\int_{B^{+}_{r}}\big(|Df|-|Df_{\varepsilon}|\big)^{2}+\int_{B^{+}_{r}}\big(|D({\bm{\eta}}\circ f)|-|D({\bm{\eta}}\circ f_{\varepsilon})|\big)^{2}\leq{\varepsilon}\,.

(7.30)

If in addition
f|_{\partial B^{+}_{r}\setminus{\mathcal{H}}_{r}}\in W^{1,2}(\partial B_{r},{\mathcal{A}}_{Q})
,
then
f_{\varepsilon}
can be chosen to satisfy also

\int_{\partial B^{+}_{r}\setminus{\mathcal{H}}_{r}}{\mathcal{G}}(f,f_{\varepsilon})^{2}+\int_{\partial B^{+}_{r}\setminus{\mathcal{H}}_{r}}\big(|Df|-|Df_{\varepsilon}|\big)^{2}\leq{\varepsilon}.

(7.31)

Now we need the following interpolation lemma.

Lemma 7.9

(Interpolation)
.

There exists a constant
C_{0}=C_{0}(n,Q)>0
with the following property. Assume
r\in]1,3\left[,f\in W^{1,2}\left(B_{r},\mathcal{A}_{Q}\right)\right.
satisfies
f|_{{\mathcal{H}}_{r}}=Q\left\llbracket{0}\right\rrbracket
and
\left.f\right|_{\partial B_{r}}\in W^{1,2}\left(\partial B_{r},\mathcal{A}_{Q}\right)
, and
g\in W^{1,2}\left(\partial B_{r}^{+},\mathcal{A}_{Q}\right)
is such that
g|_{{\mathcal{H}}_{r}\cap\partial B_{r}^{+}}=Q\left\llbracket{0}\right\rrbracket
. Then, for every
\left.\varepsilon\in\right]0,r[
, there exists a function
h_{\varepsilon}\in W^{1,2}\left(B_{r},\mathcal{A}_{Q}\right)
such that
\left.h_{\varepsilon}\right|_{\partial B_{r}}=g
,
h_{\varepsilon}|_{{\mathcal{H}}_{r}}=Q\left\llbracket{0}\right\rrbracket
and

\displaystyle\int_{B_{r}^{+}}|Dh_{\varepsilon}|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{B_{r}^{+}}|Df|^{2}+\varepsilon\int_{\partial B_{r}^{+}}\left(\left|D_{\tau}f\right|^{2}+\left|D_{\tau}g\right|^{2}\right)+\frac{C_{0}}{\varepsilon}\int_{\partial B_{r}^{+}}\mathcal{G}(f,g)^{2}\,,

(7.32)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Lip}(h_{\varepsilon})

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\left\{\operatorname{Lip}(f)+\operatorname{Lip}(g)+\varepsilon^{-1}\sup_{\partial B_{r}^{+}}\mathcal{G}(f,g)\right\}\,,

(7.33)

\displaystyle\int_{B_{r}^{+}}|\bm{\eta}\circ h_{\varepsilon}|

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\int_{\partial B_{r}^{+}}|\bm{\eta}\circ g|+C_{0}\int_{B_{r}^{+}}|\bm{\eta}\circ f|\,,

(7.34)

where
D_{\tau}
denotes the tangential derivative.

Proof.

The proof is the same as in
[
11
, Lemma 4.6]
, because the map constructed there by the linear interpolation on the annulus and taking
f
in the interior disk vanishes on
{\mathcal{H}}_{r_{1}}
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
7.8
.

We can apply directly
[
8
, Lemma 5.5]
to obtain a Lipschitz function
\tilde{f}_{\varepsilon}
satisfying
(\tilde{f}_{\varepsilon})_{|{\mathcal{H}}_{r}}=Q\left\llbracket{0}\right\rrbracket
and (
7.30
).
∎

Proof of Proposition
7.7
.

The proof goes along the same lines as the proof of
[
11
, Proposition 4.4]
using Lemmas
7.8
and
7.9
instead of
[
11
, Lemma 4.5, Lemma 4.6]
, taking into account that the situation here is simpler because we do not have translating sheets. For the sake of completeness we report here the details. Set for simplicity
A_{k}:=\left\|\mathcal{G}\left(h_{k},h\right)\right\|_{L^{2}\left(B_{r_{1}}^{+}\setminus B_{r_{0}}^{+}\right)}
and
B_{k}:=

\left\|{\bm{\eta}}\circ h_{k}\right\|_{L^{1}\left(B_{r_{1}}^{+}\right)}.
If for any
k
large enough
A_{k}\equiv 0
, then there is nothing to prove and so we can assume that, for a subsequence (not relabeled)
A_{k}>0.
In case that for yet another subsequence (not relabeled)
B_{k}>0,
we consider the function

\psi_{k}(r):=\int_{\partial B_{r}}\left(\left|Dh_{k}\right|^{2}+\left|Dh\right|^{2}\right)+A_{k}^{-2}\int_{\partial B_{r}}\mathcal{G}\left(h_{k},h\right)^{2}+B_{k}^{-1}\int_{\partial B_{r}}\left|\bm{\eta}\circ h_{k}\right|.

(7.35)

By assumption
\lim\inf_{k}\int_{r_{0}}^{r_{1}}\psi_{k}(r)dr<\infty.
Hence by Fatou’s Lemma, there is an
\left.r\in\right]r_{0},r_{1}[

and a subsequence (not relabeled) such that
\lim_{k}\psi_{k}(r)<\infty.
Thus, for some
M>0
we have

\displaystyle\int_{\partial B_{r}^{+}}\mathcal{G}\left(h_{k},h\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\rightarrow 0\,,

(7.36)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Dir}\left(h,\partial B_{r}^{+}\right)+\operatorname{Dir}\left(h_{k},\partial B_{r}^{+}\right)

\displaystyle\leq M\,,

(7.37)

\displaystyle\int_{\partial B_{r}^{+}}\left|\bm{\eta}\circ h_{k}\right|

\displaystyle\leq M\left\|\bm{\eta}\circ h_{k}\right\|_{L^{1}\left(B_{r_{1}}\right)}\,.

(7.38)

In case
B_{k}=0
for all
k
large enough, we define
\psi_{k}
by dropping the last summand in (
7.35
) and reach the same conclusion. We apply Lemma
7.8
with
f=h
,
r=r_{1}
and find a Lipschitz function
h_{\bar{\varepsilon}_{1}}
satisfying the conclusion of the lemma with
\bar{\varepsilon}_{1}=\bar{\varepsilon}_{1}(\eta,M)>0
(which will be chosen later). In particular we have

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{G}\left(h_{k},h_{\bar{\varepsilon}_{1}}\right)\right\|_{L^{2}(B_{r_{1}}^{+}\setminus B_{r_{0}}^{+})}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\mathcal{G}\left(h_{k},h\right)\right\|_{L^{2}(B_{r_{1}}^{+}\setminus B_{r_{0}}^{+})}+\left\|\mathcal{G}\left(h,h_{\bar{\varepsilon}_{1}}\right)\right\|_{L^{2}(B_{r_{1}}^{+}\setminus B_{r_{0}}^{+})}\leq o(1)+\bar{\varepsilon}_{1}\,,

\displaystyle\operatorname{Dir}\left(h_{\bar{\varepsilon}_{1}},\partial B_{r}^{+}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\operatorname{Dir}\left(h,\partial B_{r}^{+}\right)\leq M+\bar{\varepsilon}_{1}\,.

To obtain also the estimate (
7.29
), which will be required in the construction of the center manifold, we argue along the same lines of
[
11
, Proposition 4.4]
. For
h_{\bar{\varepsilon}_{1}}=\sum_{i=1}^{Q}\left\llbracket{(h_{\bar{\varepsilon}_{1}})_{i}}\right\rrbracket
we set
\bar{h}_{\bar{\varepsilon}_{1}}:=\sum_{i=1}^{Q}\left\llbracket{(h_{\bar{\varepsilon}_{1}})_{i}-{\bm{\eta}}\circ h_{\bar{\varepsilon}_{1}}+({\bm{\eta}}\circ h)*\varphi_{\rho}}\right\rrbracket
, where
\varphi_{\rho}(x):=\frac{1}{\rho^{n}}\varphi(\frac{x}{\rho})
, and
\varphi(x)=\bar{\varphi}(x-z_{0})
with
\bar{\varphi}
being the standard bump function with support in
B_{1}(0)
,
z_{0}:=(0,-2)
and
\rho
will be chosen small enough later. Observe that
{\rm spt}(\varphi_{\rho})=B_{\rho}(\rho z_{0})\subseteq B_{r}^{-}
for every
\rho
small enough and
{\rm spt}(\varphi)=B_{1}(z_{0})
. The reason to introduce this convolution kernel
\varphi_{\rho}
with support contained in
B_{r}^{-}
is that we need to preserve the zero boundary condition on
{\mathcal{H}}_{r}
. Indeed, we claim that such an
\bar{h}_{\bar{\varepsilon}_{1}}
satisfies
(\bar{h}_{\bar{\varepsilon}_{1}})|_{{\mathcal{H}}_{r}}=Q\left\llbracket{0}\right\rrbracket
in addition to all the other conclusion of the proposition. The fact that
(\bar{h}_{\varepsilon})|_{{\mathcal{H}}_{r}}=Q\left\llbracket{0}\right\rrbracket
is a simple consequence of the definitions and we leave it to the reader. Observe that the standard approximation properties of mollifiers reinterpreted suitably extends to this new kind of kernel. In particular, we can choose
\rho
small enough to have

\displaystyle Q^{2}\|{\bm{\eta}}\circ h-({\bm{\eta}}\circ h)*\varphi_{\rho}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\bar{\varepsilon}_{1}\,,

(7.39)

\displaystyle\|D({\bm{\eta}}\circ h)-D(({\bm{\eta}}\circ h)*\varphi_{\rho})\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\bar{\varepsilon}_{1}\,,

(7.40)

for some small
\bar{\varepsilon}_{1}
.
These last two inequalities combined with (
7.36
), (
7.37
), (
7.38
) imply

•

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{G}\left(h_{k},\bar{h}_{\bar{\varepsilon}_{1}}\right)\right\|_{L^{2}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:convolution}}}{{\leq}}\left\|\mathcal{G}\left(h_{k},h\right)\right\|_{L^{2}}+2\left\|\mathcal{G}\left(h,\bar{h}_{\bar{\varepsilon}_{1}}\right)\right\|_{L^{2}}+\bar{\varepsilon}_{1}\leq o(1)+3\bar{\varepsilon}_{1}\,,

•

\displaystyle\operatorname{Dir}\left(\bar{h}_{\bar{\varepsilon}_{1}},\partial B_{r}\right)\leq 2M+2\bar{\varepsilon}_{1}\,,

•

\displaystyle\operatorname{Dir}\left(\bar{h}_{\bar{\varepsilon}_{1}},B_{r}\right)=\sum_{i}\int_{B_{r}}\left|D\left(\bar{h}_{\bar{\varepsilon}_{1}}\right)_{i}-D\left(\bm{\eta}\circ\bar{h}_{\bar{\varepsilon}_{1}}\right)+D\left(({\bm{\eta}}\circ h)*\varphi_{\bar{\rho}}\right)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{B_{r}}\left(\left|D\bar{h}_{\bar{\varepsilon}_{1}}\right|^{2}-Q\left|D\left(\bm{\eta}\circ\bar{h}_{\bar{\varepsilon}_{1}}\right)\right|^{2}+Q\left|D\left(({\bm{\eta}}\circ h)*\varphi_{\bar{\rho}}\right)\right|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=Q\int_{B_{r}}\left(\left|D\left(\bm{\eta}\circ h\right)\right|^{2}-\left|D\left(\bm{\eta}\circ\bar{h}_{\bar{\varepsilon}_{1}}\right)\right|^{2}+\left|D\left(\bm{\eta}\circ h*\varphi_{\bar{\rho}}\right)\right|^{2}-\left|D\left(\bm{\eta}\circ h\right)\right|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\operatorname{Dir}\left(\bar{h}_{\bar{\varepsilon}_{1}},B_{r}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\operatorname{Dir}\left(h_{\bar{\varepsilon}_{1}},B_{r}\right)+2Q\bar{\varepsilon}_{1}\,,

where we used (
7.30
),(
7.40
) in the last inequality.
We can then apply the interpolation Lemma
7.9
with
f=\bar{h}_{\bar{\varepsilon}_{1}}
and
g={h_{k}}_{|\partial B_{r}^{+}}
, and
\varepsilon=\bar{\varepsilon}_{2}=\bar{\varepsilon}_{2}(\eta,M)>0
to get maps
H_{k}
satisfying
\left.H_{k}\right|_{\partial B_{r}^{+}}=\left.h_{k}\right|_{\partial B_{r}^{+}}
,
H_{k}|_{B^{+}_{r_{1}}\setminus B^{+}_{r}}=h_{k}|_{B^{+}_{r_{1}}\setminus B^{+}_{r}}
. Now, we use (
7.36
), (
7.37
), (
7.38
) (
7.30
) and (
7.31
) to deduce

\displaystyle\operatorname{Dir}\left(H_{k},B_{r}^{+}\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:4.14GAFA}}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle\operatorname{Dir}\left(\bar{h}_{\bar{\varepsilon}_{1}},B_{r}^{+}\right)+\bar{\varepsilon}_{2}\operatorname{Dir}\left(\bar{h}_{\bar{\varepsilon}_{1}},\partial B_{r}^{+}\right)+\bar{\varepsilon}_{2}\operatorname{Dir}\left(h_{k},\partial B_{r}^{+}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{C_{0}}{\bar{\varepsilon}_{2}}\int_{\partial B_{r}^{+}}\mathcal{G}\left(\bar{h}_{\bar{\varepsilon}_{1}},h_{k}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:approx bordo}}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle\operatorname{Dir}\left(h,B_{r}^{+}\right)+\bar{\varepsilon}_{1}+2Q\bar{\varepsilon}_{1}+3\bar{\varepsilon}_{2}\left[\operatorname{Dir}\left(h,\partial B_{r}^{+}\right)+\bar{\varepsilon}_{1}\right]+\bar{\varepsilon}_{2}M

\displaystyle+\frac{C_{0}}{\bar{\varepsilon}_{2}}\left[\int_{\partial B_{r}^{+}}\mathcal{G}\left(h,h_{k}\right)^{2}+\int_{\partial B_{r}^{+}}\mathcal{G}\left(h_{\bar{\varepsilon}_{1}},h\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\operatorname{Dir}\left(h,B_{r}^{+}\right)+\bar{\varepsilon}_{1}(1+2Q)+\bar{\varepsilon}_{2}(4M+3\bar{\varepsilon}_{1})+C_{0}\bar{\varepsilon}_{2}^{-1}\left[o(1)+\bar{\varepsilon}_{1}\right].

An appropriate choice of the parameters
\bar{\varepsilon}_{1}
and
\bar{\varepsilon}_{2}
gives the desired bound
\operatorname{Dir}\left(H_{k},B_{r}\right)\leq

\operatorname{Dir}\left(h,B_{r}\right)+\eta
for
k
large enough.
Observe next that, by construction,
\operatorname{Lip}\left(\bar{h}_{\bar{\varepsilon}_{1}}\right)
depends on
\eta
and
h
, but not on
k
. Moreover, we have

\left\|\mathcal{G}\left(\bar{h}_{\bar{\varepsilon}_{1}},h_{k}\right)\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\partial B_{r}\right)}\leq C\left\|\mathcal{G}\left(\bar{h}_{\bar{\varepsilon}_{1}},h_{k}\right)\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\partial B_{r}\right)}+C\operatorname{Lip}\left(h_{k}\right)+C\operatorname{Lip}\left(\bar{h}_{\bar{\varepsilon}_{1}}\right).

To prove the last inequality put
F(x):=\mathcal{G}\left(\bar{h}_{\bar{\varepsilon}_{1}}(x),h_{k}(x)\right)
and observe that
F(x)\leq F(y)+{\rm{Lip}}(F)|x-y|
, then integrate in
y
and use the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality combined with the fact that
{\rm{Lip}}(F)\leq C({\rm{Lip}}(\bar{h}_{\bar{\varepsilon}_{1}})+{\rm{Lip}}(h_{k}))
.
Thus (
7.27
) follows from (
7.33
). Finally, (
7.28
) follows from the Poincaré inequality applied to
\mathcal{G}\left(H_{k},h_{k}\right)
(which vanishes identically on
\left.\partial B_{r}^{+}\right)
, in fact we have

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{G}\left(H_{k},h_{k}\right)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(B_{r_{1}}^{+})}\leq C\|\nabla\mathcal{G}\left(H_{k},h_{k}\right)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(B_{r_{1}}^{+})}\leq C\textup{Dir}(h_{k},B^{+}_{r_{1}})+C\textup{Dir}(H_{k},B^{+}_{r_{1}}).

(
7.29
) follows from (
7.34
), because of (
7.38
) and
\left\|\bm{\eta}\circ\bar{h}_{\bar{\varepsilon}_{1}}\right\|_{L^{1}\left(B_{r}\right)}=\left\|\left(\bm{\eta}\circ h\right)*\varphi_{\bar{\rho}}\right\|_{L^{1}\left(B_{r}\right)}\leq\left\|\bm{\eta}\circ h\right\|_{L^{1}\left(B_{r_{1}}\right)}
if
\bar{\rho}
is also chosen small
enough such that
r+\bar{\rho}<r_{1}
. Indeed, observe that

\displaystyle\|{\bm{\eta}}\circ H_{k}\|_{L^{1}(B_{r_{1}}^{+})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|{\bm{\eta}}\circ H_{k}\|_{L^{1}(B_{r}^{+})}+\|{\bm{\eta}}\circ h_{k}\|_{L^{1}(B_{r_{1}}^{+}\setminus B_{r}^{+})}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:4.16GAFA}}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle C_{0}\int_{\partial B_{r}^{+}}|\bm{\eta}\circ h_{k}|+C_{0}\int_{B_{r}^{+}}|{\bm{\eta}}\circ\bar{h}_{\bar{\varepsilon}_{1}}|+\|{\bm{\eta}}\circ h_{k}\|_{L^{1}(B_{r_{1}}^{+}\setminus B_{r}^{+})}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:4.20GAFADS3}}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle C_{0}\|{\bm{\eta}}\circ h_{k}\|_{L^{1}(B_{r}^{+})}+C_{0}\int_{B_{r}^{+}}|({\bm{\eta}}\circ h)*\varphi_{\rho}|+\|{\bm{\eta}}\circ h_{k}\|_{L^{1}(B_{r_{1}}^{+}\setminus B_{r}^{+})}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:convolution}}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle C_{0}\|{\bm{\eta}}\circ h_{k}\|_{L^{1}(B_{r}^{+})}+C\|{\bm{\eta}}\circ h\|_{L^{1}(B_{r}^{+})}+\|{\bm{\eta}}\circ h_{k}\|_{L^{1}(B_{r_{1}}^{+}\setminus B_{r}^{+})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|{\bm{\eta}}\circ h_{k}\|_{L^{1}(B_{r_{1}}^{+})}+C\|{\bm{\eta}}\circ h\|_{L^{1}(B_{r_{1}}^{+})},

provided
\rho
is chosen so small that
\bar{r}+\rho<r
.
∎

8.
Higher integrability estimate

We consider the density
\mathbf{d}_{T}
of the measure
{\mathbf{e}}_{T}
with respect to the Lebesgue measure
|\cdot|
, i.e.

\mathbf{d}_{T}(y)=\limsup_{s\to 0}\frac{{\mathbf{e}}_{T}(B_{s}(y))}{\pi s^{2}}\,.

We will drop the subscript
T
when the current in question is clear from the context.
Clearly, under the assumptions of Proposition
6.4
,
\|\mathbf{d}_{T}\|_{L^{1}}\leq CE
. Now, following the approach of
[
11
]
, we wish to prove an
L^{p}
estimate for a
p>1
, which is just a geometric constant.

Theorem 8.1
.

There exist constants
p>1
,
C
, and
\varepsilon>0
(depending on
n
and
Q
) such that, if
T
is as in Proposition
6.4
, then

\int_{\{{\mathbf{d}}\leq 1\}\cap B_{2}}{\mathbf{d}}^{p}\leq C\,\left(E+\mathbf{A}^{2}\right)^{p}.

(8.1)

8.1.
Higher integrability for Dir-minimizers

We start with an analogous estimate for the gradient of Dir-minimizers.

Proposition 8.2
.

There are constants
q>1,\delta>0
and
C
(depending only on
Q
and
n
) with the following property. Consider a connected domain
D
in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
such that:

•

the curvature
\kappa
of
\partial D
enjoys the bound
\|\kappa\|_{\infty}\leq\delta
;

•

\partial D\cap B_{16}(x)
is connected for every
x
.

Let
0<\rho\leq 1
and
u:B_{8\rho}(x)\cap D\to{\mathcal{A}}_{Q}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})
be a Dir-minimizing function such that
u|_{\partial D\cap B_{\rho}(x)}=Q\left\llbracket{g}\right\rrbracket
for some
C^{1}
function
g
. Then

\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.98003pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.26338pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.6363pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.45924pt}}\!\int_{B_{\rho}(x)\cap D}|Du|^{2q}\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}\leq C\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.98003pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.26338pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.6363pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.45924pt}}\!\int_{B_{8\rho}(x)\cap D}|Du|^{2}+C\|Dg\|_{\infty}^{2}\,.

(8.2)

Proof.

First of all, the claim follows from
[
11
, Theorem 6.1]
when
B_{2\rho}(x)\subset D
, while it is trivial if
B_{2\rho}(x)\subset{\rm int}\,(D^{c})
. We can thus assume, without loss of generality, that
B_{2\rho}(x)
intersects
\partial D
. Let
y
be a point in such intersection and observe that
B_{\rho}(x)\subset B_{4\rho}(y)
. The claim thus follows if we can show

\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{B_{r}(y)\cap D}|Du|^{2q}\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}\leq C\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{B_{2r}(y)\cap D}|Du|^{2}+C\|Dg\|_{\infty}^{2}\,,

(8.3)

for every
y\in\partial D
and every
r\leq 4
. We now define

\bar{u}(z)=\sum_{i}\left\llbracket{u_{i}(z)-{\bm{\eta}}\circ u(z)}\right\rrbracket\,,

and observe that
|Du|\leq|D\bar{u}|+Q|D{\bm{\eta}}\circ u|
, while
{\bm{\eta}}\circ u
is a classical harmonic function such that
{\bm{\eta}}\circ u|_{\partial D\cap B_{2}}=g
, and
\bar{u}
is a Dir-minimizing function such that
\bar{u}|_{\partial D\cap B_{2}}=Q\left\llbracket{0}\right\rrbracket
. Observe that

\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{B_{r}(y)\cap D}|D{\bm{\eta}}\circ u|^{2q}\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}\leq C\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{B_{2r}(y)\cap D}|D{\bm{\eta}}\circ u|^{2}+C\|Dg\|_{\infty}^{2}

is a classical estimate for (single-valued) harmonic functions and that
|D{\bm{\eta}}\circ u|\leq|Du|
. Hence, it suffices to prove (
8.3
) when
g=Q\left\llbracket{0}\right\rrbracket
. Moreover without loss of generality we can assume that
y=0
and
r=1
. Our goal is thus to show

\||Du|\|_{L^{2q}(B_{1}\cap D)}\leq C\||Du|\|_{L^{2}(B_{2}\cap D)}\,,

under the assumption that
u|_{\partial D\cap B_{2}}=Q\left\llbracket{0}\right\rrbracket
.
If we extend
|Du|
trivially to the complement of
D
, by setting it identically equal to
0
, the inequality is just an higher integrability estimate for the function
|Du|
on
B_{1}
. By Gehring’s lemma, it suffices to prove the existence of a constant
C
such that

\||Du|\|_{L^{2}(B_{\rho}(x))}\leq C\||Du|\|_{L^{1}(B_{8\rho}(x))}

(8.4)

whenever
B_{8\rho}(x)\subset B_{2}
. However, in the “interior case”
B_{2\rho}(x)\subset D
, the stronger

\||Du|\|_{L^{2}(B_{\rho}(x))}\leq C\||Du|\|_{L^{1}(B_{2\rho}(x))}

is already proved in
[
11
, Proposition 6.2]
. Hence, arguing as above, it suffices to prove (
8.4
), with the ball
B_{4\rho}(x)
replacing
B_{\rho}(x)
in the left hand side, under the additional assumption
x\in\partial D
. Again by scaling, we are reduced to prove the following estimate

\||Du|\|_{L^{2}(B_{1}\cap D)}\leq C\||Du|\|_{L^{1}(B_{2}\cap D)}\qquad\mbox{if $0\in\partial D$.}

(8.5)

First of all observe that, by our assumptions, if
\delta
is sufficiently small, for every
r\in(1,2)
the domain
D\cap B_{r}
is biLipschitz equivalent to the half disk
B_{r}\cap\{(x_{1},x_{2}):x_{2}>0\}
, with uniform bounds on the Lipschitz constants of the homeomorphism and its inverse. In particular, we recall that, by classical Sobolev space theory, we have

\min_{c\in\mathbb{R}}\|f-c\|_{H^{1/2}(\partial(B_{r}\cap D))}\leq C\|Df\|_{L^{1}(\partial(B_{r}\cap D))}

for every classical function
f\in W^{1,1}(\partial B_{r},\mathbb{R})
.
Moreover there is an extension
F\in W^{1,2}(B_{r}\cap D)
of
f
such that

\|DF\|_{L^{2}(B_{r}\cap D)}\leq C\|f-c\|_{H^{1/2}(\partial(B_{r}\cap D))}\leq C\|Df\|_{L^{1}(\partial(B_{r}\cap D))}\,.

(8.6)

Thus, using Fubini and (
8.6
), under our assumptions on
u
, we find a radius
r\in(1,2)
and an extension
v
of the classical function
{\bm{\xi}}\circ u|_{\partial(B_{r}\cap D)}
to
B_{r}\cap D
such that

\|D{\bm{\xi}}\circ u\|_{L^{2}(B_{r}\cap D)}\leq C\|D{\bm{\xi}}\circ u\|_{L^{1}(\partial(B_{r}\cap D))}\leq C\|D{\bm{\xi}}\circ u\|_{L^{1}(B_{2}\cap D)}\leq C\\
|Du|\|_{L^{1}(D\cap B_{2})}\,.

(8.7)

If we consider the multivalued function
{\bm{\xi}}^{-1}\circ{\bm{\rho}}\circ v
, the latter has trace
w:={\bm{\xi}}^{-1}\circ{\bm{\xi}}\circ u
on
\partial(B_{r}\cap D)
. Therefore, by minimality of
u
,

\||Du|\|_{L^{2}(B_{r}\cap D)}\leq\|Dw\|_{L^{2}(B_{r}\cap D)}\leq C\|Dv\|_{L^{2}(B_{r}\cap D)}\,.

Combining the latter inequality with (
8.7
) we achieve (
8.5
).
∎

8.2.
Improved excess estimates

Proposition 8.3

(Weak excess estimate)
.

For every
\eta>0,
there exists
\varepsilon>
0 with the following property. Let
T
be area minimizing and assume it satisfies Assumption
6.2
in
\mathbf{C}_{4s}(x).
If
E=\mathbf{E}\left(T,\mathbf{C}_{4s}(x)\right)\leq\varepsilon,
then

\mathbf{e}_{T}(A)\leq\eta_{10}Es^{2}+C\mathbf{A}^{2}s^{4}

(8.8)

for every
A\subset B_{s}(x)\cap D
Borel with
|A|\leq\varepsilon\left|B_{s}(x)\right|
.

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we can assume
s=1
and
x=0.
We distinguish the two regimes:
E\leq\mathbf{A}^{2}
and
\mathbf{A}^{2}\leq E.
In the former, clearly
\mathbf{e}_{T}(A)\leq CE\leq C\mathbf{A}^{2}.
In the latter, we let
f
be the
E^{\frac{1}{8}}
-Lipschitz approximation of
T
in
\mathbf{C}_{3}
and, arguing as for the proof of
[
11
, Theorem 5.2]
we find a radius
r\in(1,2)
and a current
R
such that

\partial R=\left\langle T-\mathbf{G}_{f},\varphi,r\right\rangle

and

\mathbf{M}(R)\leq\left(\frac{C}{\delta_{*}}(E+\mathbf{A}^{2}r^{2})\right)^{2}\leq CE^{2-\frac{1}{2}}.

Therefore, by the Taylor expansion in Remark
5.4,
we have:

\displaystyle\|T\|\left(\mathbf{C}_{r}\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle minimality}}{{\leq}}\mathbf{M}\left(\mathbf{G}_{f}\mathop{\hbox{\vrule height=7.0pt,width=0.3pt,depth=0.0pt\vrule height=0.3pt,width=5.0pt,depth=0.0pt}}\nolimits\mathbf{C}_{r}+R\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle triangular}}{{\leq}}\left\|\mathbf{G}_{f}\right\|\left(\mathbf{C}_{r}\right)+CE^{\frac{3}{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\stackrel{{\scriptstyle Taylor}}{{\leq}}Q\left|B_{r}\right|+\int_{B_{r}}\frac{|Df|^{2}}{2}+CE^{\frac{5}{4}}\,.

(8.9)

On the other hand, using again the Taylor expansion for the part of the current which coincides with the graph of
f,
we deduce as well that

\|T\|\left(\left(B_{r}\cap K\right)\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\right)\geq Q\left|B_{r}\cap K\right|+\int_{B_{r}\cap K}\frac{|Df|^{2}}{2}-CE^{\frac{5}{4}}.

(8.10)

Subtracting (
8.10
) from (
8.9
), we deduce

\mathbf{e}_{T}\left(B_{r}\cap D\setminus K\right)\leq\int_{B_{r}\cap D\setminus K}\frac{|Df|^{2}}{2}+CE^{\frac{5}{4}}.

(8.11)

If
\varepsilon
is chosen small enough, we infer from (
8.11
) and (
7.1
) in Theorem
7.3
that

\mathbf{e}_{T}\left(B_{r}\cap D\setminus K\right)\leq\bar{\eta}E+CE^{1+\gamma},

for a suitable
\bar{\eta}>0
to be chosen. Let now
A\subset B_{1}
be such that
|A|\leq\varepsilon\pi
. If
\varepsilon
is small enough, we can again apply Theorem
7.3
and so by (
8.2
) there is a Dir-minimizing
w
such that
|Df|
is close in
L^{2}
(with an error
\bar{\eta}E)
to
|Dw|
and by
[
11
, Remark 5.5]

\operatorname{Dir}(w)\leq CE.
By Proposition
8.2
we have

\||Dw|\|_{L^{q}\left(B_{1}\right)}\leq CE^{\frac{1}{2}}
. Therefore we can deduce

\displaystyle\mathbf{e}_{T}(A)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e:few energy},\eqref{e:quasiarm}}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle\int_{A}|Dw|^{2}+3\eta E+CE^{1+\gamma}

(8.12)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|Dg\|_{\infty}^{2}|A|^{1-2/q}+C\left(|A|^{1-2/q}+\bar{\eta}\right)E+CE^{1+\gamma}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\left(|A|^{1-2/q}+\bar{\eta}\right)E+CE^{\frac{5}{4}}.

Hence, if
\varepsilon
and
\eta
are suitably chosen, (
8.8
) follows from (
8.12
).
∎

8.3.
Proof of Theorem
8.1

The proof follows from Proposition
8.3
arguing exactly as in
[
11
, Section 6.3]
.

9.
Strong Lipschitz approximation

In this section we show how Theorem
8.1
gives a simple proof of the following approximation result analogous to
[
11
, Theorem 2.4]
.

Theorem 9.1

(Boundary Almgren Strong Approximation)
.

There are geometric constants
\gamma_{1}>0
,
\varepsilon_{A}>0
, and
C>0
with the following properties.
Let
T
and
\Gamma
be as in Assumption
6.2
with
\varepsilon=\varepsilon_{A}
, let
f
be the
E^{\gamma}
-Lipschitz approximation and
K\subset B_{3r}
the corresponding set where
\mathbf{G}_{f}
and
T
coincide. Then:

\displaystyle{\rm{Lip}}(f)

\displaystyle\leq C(E+r^{2}\mathbf{A}^{2})^{\gamma_{1}}

(9.1)

\displaystyle{\rm osc}\,(f)

\displaystyle\leq C\mathbf{h}(T,{\mathbf{C}}_{4r})+Cr(E+r^{2}\mathbf{A}^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}

(9.2)

\displaystyle|B_{r}\setminus K|+{\mathbf{e}}_{T}(B_{r}\setminus K)

\displaystyle\leq Cr^{2}(E+r^{2}\mathbf{A}^{2})^{1+\gamma_{1}}

(9.3)

\displaystyle\left|\|T\|(A\times\mathbb{R}^{n})-Q|A\cap D|-\frac{1}{2}\int_{A}|Df|^{2}\right|

\displaystyle\leq Cr^{2}(E+r^{2}\mathbf{A}^{2})^{1+\gamma_{1}}

(9.4)

for every closed set
A\subset B_{r}
.

We postpone the proof till the end of this section however we anticipate that it goes along the same line of
[
11
, Theorem 2.4]
using Theorems
9.2
and
9.4
below instead of
[
11
, Theorem 7.1]
and
[
11
, Theorem 7.3]
respectively. The substantial changes necessary to adapt the argument of the interior case, i.e.,
[
11
, Theorem 2.4]
concerns mainly the proof of Theorem
9.4
while the proof of Theorem
9.2
is essentially the same as that of
[
11
, Theorem 7.1]
. So we start by stating the Almgren’s boundary strong excess estimate.

Theorem 9.2

(Almgren’s boundary strong excess estimate)
.

There are constants
\varepsilon_{11},\gamma_{11},C>0
(depending on
n,Q)
with the following property. Assume
T
satisfies Assumption
6.2
in
{\mathbf{C}}_{4}
and is area minimizing. If
E=\mathbf{E}\left(T,{\mathbf{C}}_{4}\right)<\varepsilon_{11},
then

\mathbf{e}_{T}(A)\leq C\left((E+\mathbf{A}^{2})^{\gamma_{11}}+|A|^{\gamma_{11}}\right)\left(E+\mathbf{A}^{2}\right),

(9.5)

for every Borel set
A\subset B_{\frac{9}{8}}
.

This estimate complements (
8.1
) enabling to control the excess also in the region where
\mathbf{d}>1
. We call it boundary strong Almgren’s estimate because a similar formula in the interior case can be found in the big regularity paper (cf.
[
6
, Sections 3.24-3.26 and 3.30(8)]
) and is a strengthened version of Proposition
8.3
that we called weak excess estimate. To prove (
9.5
) we construct a suitable competitor to estimate the size of the set
\tilde{K}
where the graph of the
E^{\beta}
Lipschitz approximation
f
differs from
T
. Following Almgren, we embed
{\mathcal{A}}_{Q}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})
in a large Euclidean space, via a bilipschitz embedding
{\bm{\xi}}
. We then regularize
{\bm{\xi}}\circ f
by convolution and project it back onto
\mathcal{Q}={\bm{\xi}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{Q}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})\right).
To avoid loss of energy we need a rather special ”almost projection”
\bm{\rho}_{\delta}^{\star}
that preserves zero boundary data, i.e.,
\bm{\rho}_{\delta}^{\star}(0)=0
.

Proposition 9.3
.

(
[
11
, Proposition 7.2]
)
For every
n,Q\in\mathbb{N}\setminus\{0\}
there are geometric constants
\delta_{0},C>
0 with the following property. For every
\delta\in]0,\delta_{0}\left[\right.
there is
\bm{\rho}_{\delta}^{\star}:\mathbb{R}^{N(Q,n)}\rightarrow\mathcal{Q}=

{\bm{\xi}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{Q}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})\right)
such that
\bm{\rho}_{\delta}^{\star}(0)=0
,
\left|\bm{\rho}_{\delta}^{\star}(P)-P\right|\leq C\delta^{8^{-nQ}}
for all
P\in\mathcal{Q}
and, for every
u\in W^{1,2}\left(\Omega,\mathbb{R}^{N}\right)
, the following holds:

\int\left|D\left(\bm{\rho}_{\delta}^{\star}\circ u\right)\right|^{2}\leq\left(1+C\delta^{8^{-nQ-1}}\right)\int_{\left\{\operatorname{dist}(u,Q)\leq\delta^{nQ+1}\right\}}|Du|^{2}+C\int_{\left\{\operatorname{dist}(u,Q)>\delta^{nQ+1}\right\}}|Du|^{2}.

(9.6)

Proof.

\rho_{\delta}^{\star}
is the projection obtained in
[
11
, Proposition 7.2]
.∎

Here we show the Strong Excess Approximation of Almgren in our version that takes into account the non-homogeneous boundary value problem, concluding in this way the proof of Theorem
9.1
. Theorem
8.1
enters crucially in the argument when estimating the second summand of (
9.6
) for the regularization of
{\bm{\xi}}\circ f
.

9.1.
Regularization by convolution with a non centered kernel

Here we construct the competitor preserving the boundary conditions.

Proposition 9.4
.

Let
\beta_{1}\in\left(0,\frac{1}{4}\right)
and
T
be an area minimizing current satisfying Assumption
6.2
in
\mathbf{C}_{4}.
Let
f
be its
E^{\beta_{1}}
-Lipschitz approximation. Then, there exist constants
\bar{\varepsilon}_{12},\gamma_{12},C>0
and a subset of radii
B\subset[9/8,2]
with
|B|>1/2
with the following properties. If
\mathbf{E}\left(T,\mathbf{C}_{4}\right)\leq\bar{\varepsilon}_{12},
for every
\sigma\in B,
there exists a
Q
-valued function
h\in\operatorname{Lip}\left(B_{\sigma}\cap D,{\mathcal{A}}_{Q}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})\right)
such that

\displaystyle h|_{B_{\sigma}\cap\partial D}

\displaystyle=g,

\displaystyle h|_{\partial B_{\sigma}\cap D}

\displaystyle=f|_{\partial B_{\sigma}\cap D},

\displaystyle\operatorname{Lip}(h)

\displaystyle\leq C(E+\mathbf{A}^{2})^{\beta_{1}},

and

\int_{B_{\sigma}\cap D}|Dh|^{2}\leq\int_{B_{\sigma}\cap K\cap D}|Df|^{2}+C\left(E+\mathbf{A}^{2}\right)^{1+\gamma_{12}}.

(9.7)

Proof.

Since
|Df|^{2}\leq C\mathbf{d}_{T}\leq CE^{2\beta_{1}}\leq 1
on
K,
by Theorem
8.1
there
is
q_{1}=2p_{1}>2
such that

\||Df|\|_{L^{q_{1}}\left(K\cap B_{2}\right)}^{2}\leq C\left(E+\mathbf{A}^{2}\right).

(9.8)

Given two (vector-valued) functions
h_{1}
and
h_{2}
and two radii
0<\bar{r}<r,
we denote by
\operatorname{lin}\left(h_{1},h_{2}\right)
the linear interpolation in
B_{r}\setminus\bar{B}_{\bar{r}}
between
\left.h_{1}\right|_{\partial B_{r}}
and
\left.h_{2}\right|_{\partial B_{\bar{r}}}.
More
precisely, if
(\theta,t)\in\mathbb{S}_{+}^{m-1}\times[0,\infty)
are spherical coordinates, then

\operatorname{lin}\left(h_{1},h_{2}\right)(\theta,t)=\frac{r-t}{r-\bar{r}}h_{2}(\theta,t)+\frac{t-\bar{r}}{r-\bar{r}}h_{1}(\theta,t).

Next, let
\delta>0
and
\varepsilon>0
be two parameters and let
1<r_{1}<r_{2}<r_{3}<2
be three radii, all to be chosen suitably later. First of all extend the function
g
to the whole disk
B_{3}
by making it coinstant in the direction
x_{2}
, i.e.
g(x_{1},x_{2})=g(x_{1},\psi_{1}(x_{1}))
.
We then extend the
E^{\beta_{1}}
-Lipschitz approximation to a function
f^{*}
defined on the entire
B_{3}
by setting

f^{*}(x)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}f(x)&\mbox{if $x\in B_{3}\cap D$}\\
Q\left\llbracket{g(x)}\right\rrbracket&\mbox{if $x\in B_{3}\cap D^{-}$}\,.\end{array}\right.

From now to keep our notation simpler we denote
f^{*}
as well by
f
. Observe moreover that

\left.(\bm{\eta}\circ f)\right|_{D^{-}}=g\,.

We next define a translation operator

\oplus:A_{Q}\left(\mathbb{R}^{N}\right)\times\mathbb{R}^{N}\rightarrow\mathcal{A}_{Q}\left(\mathbb{R}^{N}\right)
setting

\quad T\oplus t=\sum_{i=1}^{Q}\left\llbracket{t_{i}+t}\right\rrbracket\qquad\mbox{for }\;\;T=\sum_{i=1}^{Q}\left\llbracket{t_{i}}\right\rrbracket\,.

We then introduce

\tilde{f}:=f\oplus(-{\bm{\eta}}\circ f)
, so that

\tilde{f}|_{D^{-}}=Q\left\llbracket{0}\right\rrbracket
and
\bm{\eta}\circ\tilde{f}=0
.

Next we define, as in the proof of Proposition
7.7
,
\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x):=\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{n}}\varphi(\frac{x}{\varepsilon})
, and
\varphi(x)=\bar{\varphi}(x-z_{0})
with
\bar{\varphi}
being the standard bump function with support in
B_{1}(0)
and
z_{0}:=(0,-2)
.
We therefore set

\tilde{h}_{\varepsilon}:=(\bm{\eta}\circ f)*\varphi_{\varepsilon}-g*\varphi_{\varepsilon}+g\,.

We easily see that
(\tilde{h}_{\varepsilon})|_{\partial D\cap B_{r_{3}}}=g|_{\partial D\cap B_{r_{3}}}
, and

{\rm{Lip}}(\tilde{h}_{\varepsilon})\leq C(E+\mathbf{A}^{2})^{\beta_{1}}\,.

Recall the maps
\bm{\rho}_{\delta}^{\star}
and
{\bm{\xi}}
of
[\mathrm{LS}11\mathrm{~b},
Theorem 2.1
]
and observe that
{\bm{\xi}}(Q\left\llbracket{0}\right\rrbracket)=0
and
\bm{\rho}_{\delta}^{\star}(0_{\mathbb{R}^{n}})=0_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}
.
We then set
\tilde{f}^{\prime}_{1}:={\bm{\xi}}\circ\tilde{f}

\tilde{g}^{\prime}_{\delta,\varepsilon,s}:=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\sqrt{E+\mathbf{A}^{2}}\bm{\rho}\circ\Phi\circ\operatorname{lin}\left(\frac{\tilde{f}_{1}^{\prime}\circ\Phi^{-1}}{\sqrt{E+\mathbf{A}^{2}}},\bm{\rho}^{\star}_{\delta}\left(\frac{\tilde{f}_{1}^{\prime}\circ\Phi^{-1}}{\sqrt{E+\mathbf{A}^{2}}}\right)\right),&\text{ in }(B_{r_{3}}\setminus B_{r_{2}})\cap D,\\
\sqrt{E+\mathbf{A}^{2}}\bm{\rho}\circ\Phi\circ\operatorname{lin}\left(\bm{\rho}_{\delta}^{\star}\left(\frac{\tilde{f}_{1}^{\prime}\circ\Phi^{-1}}{\sqrt{E+\mathbf{A}^{2}}}\right),\bm{\rho}_{\delta}^{\star}\left((\frac{\tilde{f}_{1}^{\prime}*\varphi_{\varepsilon})\circ\Phi^{-1}}{\sqrt{E+\mathbf{A}^{2}}}\right)\right),&\text{ in }(B_{r_{2}}\setminus B_{r_{1}})\cap D,\\
\sqrt{E+\mathbf{A}^{2}}\bm{\rho}_{\delta}^{\star}\left(\frac{\tilde{f}_{1}^{\prime}*\varphi_{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{E+\mathbf{A}^{2}}}\right),&\text{ in }B_{r_{1}}\cap D,\end{array}\right.

where
\Phi
is the diffeomorphism constructed in Proposition
7.6
.
Now, we define

\hat{h}_{\delta,\varepsilon,s}:=\sum_{i=1}^{Q}\left\llbracket{\left({\bm{\xi}}^{-1}\circ\tilde{g}^{\prime}_{\delta,\varepsilon,s}\right)_{i}-{\bm{\eta}}\circ\left({\bm{\xi}}^{-1}\circ\tilde{g}^{\prime}_{\delta,\varepsilon,s}\right)}\right\rrbracket,\text{ in }B_{r_{3}}\cap D,

(9.9)

and

h_{\delta,\varepsilon,s}:=\sum_{i=1}^{Q}\left\llbracket{\left({\bm{\xi}}^{-1}\circ\tilde{g}^{\prime}_{\delta,\varepsilon,s}\right)_{i}-{\bm{\eta}}\circ\left({\bm{\xi}}^{-1}\circ\tilde{g}^{\prime}_{\delta,\varepsilon,s}\right)+\tilde{h}_{\varepsilon}}\right\rrbracket,\text{ in }B_{r_{3}}\cap D.

(9.10)

Notice that the convolution of any function
u
satisfying
u_{|B_{3}\setminus D}\equiv 0
with
\varphi_{\varepsilon}
for
\varepsilon
small enough always produces smooth function
u*\varphi_{\varepsilon}
satisfying
(u*\varphi_{\varepsilon})_{|B_{3}\setminus D}\equiv 0
, because we have assumed that
\partial D
is the graph of a Lipschitz function and so it stays inside a cone with fixed angles. With this last fact in mind it is easy to see that
(\tilde{g}^{\prime}_{\delta})_{|\partial D}=0
, and
(h_{\delta})_{|\partial D}=g
,
{\bm{\eta}}\circ\hat{h}_{\delta,\varepsilon,s}=0
. We will prove that, for
\sigma:=r_{3}
in a suitable set
B\subset[9/8,2]
with
|B|>1/2,
we can choose
r_{2}=r_{3}-s
and
r_{1}=r_{2}-s
so that
h
satisfies the conclusion of the proposition. Our choice of the parameters will imply the following inequalities:

\delta^{2\cdot 8^{-nQ}}\leq s,\quad\varepsilon\leq s,\quad\text{ and }\quad E^{1-2\beta_{1}}\leq\varepsilon^{2}.

(9.11)

We estimate the Lipschitz constant of
\tilde{g}^{\prime}_{\delta}
. This can be easily done observing that

•

in
B_{r_{1}}\cap D
, we have

\operatorname{Lip}\left(\tilde{g}_{\delta}^{\prime}\right)\leq C\operatorname{Lip}\left(\tilde{f}_{1}^{\prime}*\varphi_{\varepsilon}\right)\leq C\operatorname{Lip}\left(\tilde{f}_{1}^{\prime}\right)\leq C(E+\mathbf{A}^{2})^{\beta_{1}},

•

in
(B_{r_{2}}\setminus B_{r_{1}})\cap D
, we have

\operatorname{Lip}\left(\tilde{g}_{\delta}^{\prime}\right)\leq C\operatorname{Lip}\left(\tilde{f}_{1}^{\prime}\right)+C\frac{\left\|\tilde{f}_{1}^{\prime}-\tilde{f}_{1}^{\prime}*\varphi_{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}}{s}\leq C\left(1+\frac{\varepsilon}{s}\right)\operatorname{Lip}\left(\tilde{f}_{1}^{\prime}\right)\leq C(E+\mathbf{A}^{2})^{\beta_{1}},

•

in
(B_{r_{3}}\setminus B_{r_{2}})\cap D
, we have

\operatorname{Lip}\left(\tilde{g}_{\delta}^{\prime}\right)\ \leq\ C\operatorname{Lip}\left(\tilde{f}_{1}^{\prime}\right)+C(E+\mathbf{A}^{2})^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\frac{\delta^{8^{-nQ}}}{s}\ \leq\ CE^{\beta_{1}}+C(E+\mathbf{A}^{2})^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\ \leq\ C(E+\mathbf{A}^{2})^{\beta_{1}}\,.

(9.12)

In the first inequality of the last line we have used that, since
\mathcal{Q}
is a cone,
(E+\mathbf{A}^{2})^{-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\tilde{f}_{1}^{\prime}(x)\in

\mathcal{Q}
for every
x
, hence

\left|\bm{\rho}_{\delta}^{\star}\left(\frac{\tilde{f}_{1}^{\prime}}{\sqrt{E+\mathbf{A}^{2}}}\right)-\frac{\tilde{f}_{1}^{\prime}}{\sqrt{E+\mathbf{A}^{2}}}\right|\leq C\delta^{8^{-nQ}}\,.

From (
9.12
) and (
9.11
) we deduce easily that
\tilde{g}_{\delta}^{\prime}
is continuous and piecewise Lipschitz and so globally Lipschitz and furthermore that

{\rm{Lip}}(h_{\delta,\varepsilon,s})\leq C(E+\mathbf{A}^{2})^{\beta_{1}}.

(9.13)

In the following Steps
1
-
3
we estimate the Dirichlet energy of
h_{\delta,\varepsilon,s}
and finally in Step
4
we obtain the desired estimate (
9.7
) of Theorem
9.4
for a suitable choice of
\delta,\varepsilon,s
depending on some powers of the infinitesimal quantity
E
(see (
9.39
) below). Before we realize this program, we recall that for every
f\in W^{1,2}(\Omega,{\mathcal{A}}_{Q}({\mathbb{R}}^{n}))
we have

0\leq\textup{Dir}(f\oplus(-{\bm{\eta}}\circ f))=\textup{Dir}(f)-Q\textup{Dir}({\bm{\eta}}\circ f).

(9.14)

We write here the estimate of the Dirichlet energy of
\tilde{h}_{\varepsilon}
which will be useful in combination with (
9.14
).

\displaystyle\int\left|Dg*\varphi_{\varepsilon}-Dg\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\mathbf{A}^{2}\varepsilon^{2},

(9.15)

\displaystyle\left\|Dg*\varphi_{\varepsilon}-Dg\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|D^{2}g\|_{\infty}\varepsilon\ \leq\ C\mathbf{A}\varepsilon,

\displaystyle\left|\int\left(Dg*\varphi_{\varepsilon}-Dg\right)\left(D({\bm{\eta}}\circ f)*\varphi_{\varepsilon}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\mathbf{A}\varepsilon\int|D({\bm{\eta}}\circ f)*\varphi_{\varepsilon}|

(9.16)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{Rem.}\ref{r:5.5DS3}}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle C\mathbf{A}\varepsilon(E+\mathbf{A}^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{Young}}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle C\varepsilon(E+\mathbf{A}^{2})\,.

Summing (
9.16
), (
9.15
), we obtain

\displaystyle\int|D\tilde{h}_{\varepsilon}|^{2}

\displaystyle=\int|D({\bm{\eta}}\circ f)*\varphi_{\varepsilon}|^{2}+\int\left|Dg*\varphi_{\varepsilon}-Dg\right|^{2}-2\int\left(Dg*\varphi_{\varepsilon}-Dg\right)\left(D({\bm{\eta}}\circ f)*\varphi_{\varepsilon}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\int|D({\bm{\eta}}\circ f)|^{2}+C\mathbf{A}^{2}\varepsilon^{2}+C\varepsilon(E+\mathbf{A}^{2})

\displaystyle\leq C\int|Df|^{2}+C\varepsilon(E+\mathbf{A}^{2})\,.

Step 1. Energy in

B_{r_{3}}\setminus B_{r_{2}}
. By Proposition
9.3
, we have
\left|\bm{\rho}_{\delta}^{\star}(P)-P\right|\leq

C\delta^{8^{-nQ}}
for all
P\in\mathcal{Q}:={\bm{\xi}}({\mathcal{A}}_{Q}({\mathbb{R}}^{n}))
. Thus, elementary estimates on the linear interpolation give

\displaystyle\int_{(B_{r_{3}}\setminus B_{r_{2}})\cap D}\left|D\tilde{g}_{\delta}^{\prime}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C(E+\mathbf{A}^{2})}{\left(r_{3}-r_{2}\right)^{2}}\int_{(B_{r_{3}}\setminus B_{r_{2}})\cap D}\left|\frac{\tilde{f}_{1}^{\prime}}{\sqrt{E+\mathbf{A}^{2}}}-\bm{\rho}_{\delta}^{\star}\left(\frac{\tilde{f}_{1}^{\prime}}{\sqrt{E+\mathbf{A}^{2}}}\right)\right|^{2}

(9.17)

\displaystyle+C\int_{(B_{r_{3}}\setminus B_{r_{2}})\cap D}\big|D\tilde{f}_{1}^{\prime}\big|^{2}+C\int_{(B_{r_{3}}\setminus B_{r_{2}})\cap D}\left|D\left(\bm{\rho}_{\delta}^{\star}\circ\tilde{f}_{1}^{\prime}\right)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\int_{(B_{r_{3}}\setminus B_{r_{2}})\cap D}\big|D\tilde{f}_{1}^{\prime}\big|^{2}+C(E+\mathbf{A}^{2})s^{-1}\delta^{2\cdot 8^{-nQ}}.

Hence, using that
{\rm{Lip}}({\bm{\xi}})\leq 1
and (
9.14
), we estimate

\displaystyle\int_{(B_{r_{3}}\setminus B_{r_{2}})\cap D}\left|Dh_{\delta,\varepsilon,s}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{(B_{r_{3}}\setminus B_{r_{2}})\cap D}\left|D\hat{h}_{\delta,\varepsilon,s}\right|^{2}+Q\int_{(B_{r_{3}}\setminus B_{r_{2}})\cap D}\left|D\tilde{h}_{\varepsilon}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{(B_{r_{3}}\setminus B_{r_{2}})\cap D}\left|D\tilde{g}_{\delta}^{\prime}\right|^{2}-Q\int{\bm{\eta}}+C\int_{(B_{r_{3}}\setminus B_{r_{2}})\cap D}\left|D\tilde{h}_{\varepsilon}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{(B_{r_{3}}\setminus B_{r_{2}})\cap D}\left|Df\right|^{2}+C(E+\mathbf{A}^{2})\left(\varepsilon+s^{-1}\delta^{2\cdot 8^{-nQ}}\right).

(9.18)

Step 2. Energy in

B_{r_{2}}\setminus B_{r_{1}}
. Here, using the same interpolation inequality and a standard estimate on convolutions of
W^{1,2}
functions, we get

\displaystyle\int_{(B_{r_{2}}\setminus B_{r_{1}})\cap D}\left|D\tilde{g}_{\delta}^{\prime}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{(B_{r_{2}+\varepsilon}\setminus B_{r_{1}-\varepsilon})\cap D}\left|D\tilde{f}_{1}^{\prime}\right|^{2}+\frac{CC_{\Phi}}{\left(r_{2}-r_{1}\right)^{2}}\int_{B_{r_{2}}\setminus B_{r_{1}}}\left|\tilde{f}_{1}^{\prime}-\varphi_{\varepsilon}*\tilde{f}_{1}^{\prime}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq CC_{\Phi}\int_{(B_{r_{2}+\varepsilon}\setminus B_{r_{1}-\varepsilon})\cap D}\big|D\tilde{f}_{1}^{\prime}\big|^{2}+CC_{\Phi}\varepsilon^{2}s^{-2}\int_{B_{3}\cap D}\big|D\tilde{f}_{1}^{\prime}\big|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{(B_{r_{2}+\varepsilon}\setminus B_{r_{1}-\varepsilon})\cap D}\big|D\tilde{f}_{1}^{\prime}\big|^{2}+C\varepsilon^{2}(E+\mathbf{A}^{2})s^{-2}

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{(B_{r_{2}+\varepsilon}\setminus B_{r_{1}-\varepsilon})\cap D}\left|Df\right|^{2}+C\varepsilon^{2}(E+\mathbf{A}^{2})s^{-2}.

So coming back to the energy estimate on
h_{\delta,\varepsilon,s}
we get

\displaystyle\int_{(B_{r_{2}}\setminus B_{r_{1}})\cap D}\left|Dh_{\delta,\varepsilon,s}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{(B_{r_{2}}\setminus B_{r_{1}})\cap D}\left|D\hat{h}_{\delta,\varepsilon,s}\right|^{2}+Q\int_{(B_{r_{2}}\setminus B_{r_{1}})\cap D}\left|D\tilde{h}_{\varepsilon}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{(B_{r_{2}}\setminus B_{r_{1}})\cap D}\left|D\tilde{g}_{\delta}^{\prime}\right|^{2}+C\int_{(B_{r_{2}}\setminus B_{r_{1}})\cap D}\left|D\tilde{h}_{\varepsilon}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{(B_{r_{2}+\varepsilon}\setminus B_{r_{1}-\varepsilon})\cap D}\left|Df\right|^{2}+C\varepsilon^{2}(E+\mathbf{A}^{2})s^{-2}+C\varepsilon(E+\mathbf{A}^{2})\,.

(9.19)

Step 3. Energy in

B_{r_{1}}.
Define
Z:=\left\{\operatorname{dist}\left(\frac{\tilde{f}_{1}^{\prime}}{\sqrt{E}}*\varphi_{\varepsilon},\mathcal{Q}\right)>\delta^{nQ+1}\right\}\subseteq D
and use (
9.6
)
to get

\displaystyle\int_{B_{r_{1}}\cap D}\left|D\tilde{g}_{\delta}^{\prime}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left(1+C\delta^{8^{-\bar{n}Q-1}}\right)\int_{(B_{r_{1}}\cap D)\setminus Z}\left|D\left(\tilde{f}_{1}^{\prime}*\varphi_{\varepsilon}\right)\right|^{2}+C\int_{Z}\left|D\left(\tilde{f}_{1}^{\prime}*\varphi_{\varepsilon}\right)\right|^{2}

(9.20)

\displaystyle=:I_{1}+I_{2}.

We consider
I_{1}
and
I_{2}
separately. For
I_{1}
we first observe the elementary inequality

\displaystyle\left\|D\left(\tilde{f}_{1}^{\prime}*\varphi_{\varepsilon}\right)\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|(D\tilde{f}_{1}^{\prime})*\varphi_{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\left(\big|D\tilde{f}_{1}^{\prime}\big|\mathbf{1}_{K}\right)*\varphi_{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\left(\big|D\tilde{f}_{1}^{\prime}\big|\mathbf{1}_{K^{c}}\right)*\varphi_{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+2\left\|\left(\big|D\tilde{f}_{1}^{\prime}\big|\mathbf{1}_{K}\right)*\varphi_{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}}\left\|\left(\big|D\tilde{f}_{1}^{\prime}\big|\mathbf{1}_{K^{c}}\right)*\varphi_{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}},

(9.21)

where
K^{c}
is the complement of
K
in
D
. Recalling
r_{1}+\varepsilon\leq r_{1}+s=r_{2}
we estimate the first summand in (
9.21
) as follows:

\left\|\left(\big|D\tilde{f}_{1}^{\prime}\big|\mathbf{1}_{K}\right)*\varphi_{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}\left(B_{r_{1}}\cap D\right)}^{2}\leq\int_{B_{r_{1}+\varepsilon}\cap D}\left(\big|D\tilde{f}_{1}^{\prime}\big|\mathbf{1}_{K}\right)^{2}\leq\int_{B_{r_{2}}\cap K}\big|D\tilde{f}_{1}^{\prime}\big|^{2}.

(9.22)

In order to treat the other terms, recall that
\operatorname{Lip}\left(\tilde{f}_{1}^{\prime}\right)\leq C(E+\mathbf{A}^{2})^{\beta_{1}}
and
\left|K^{c}\right|\leq C(E+\mathbf{A}^{2})^{1-2\beta_{1}}
. Thus, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\big|D\tilde{f}_{1}^{\prime}\big|\mathbf{1}_{K^{c}}\right)*\varphi_{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}\left(B_{r_{1}}\cap D\right)}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(E+\mathbf{A}^{2})^{2\beta_{1}}\left\|\mathbf{1}_{K^{c}}*\varphi_{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(E+\mathbf{A}^{2})^{2\beta_{1}}\left\|\mathbf{1}_{K^{c}}\right\|_{L^{1}}^{2}\left\|\varphi_{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq\frac{C(E+\mathbf{A}^{2})^{2-2\beta_{1}}}{\varepsilon^{2}}.

(9.23)

Putting (
9.22
) and (
9.23
) in (
9.21
) and recalling
(E+\mathbf{A}^{2})^{1-2\beta_{1}}\leq\varepsilon^{2}
and
\int\big|D\tilde{f}_{1}^{\prime}\big|^{2}\leq C(E+\mathbf{A}^{2})
, we get

I_{1}\leq\int_{B_{r_{2}}\cap K}\big|D\tilde{f}_{1}^{\prime}\big|^{2}+C\delta^{8^{-\bar{n}Q-1}}(E+\mathbf{A}^{2})+C\varepsilon^{-1}(E+\mathbf{A}^{2})^{3/2-\beta_{1}}.

(9.24)

For what concerns
I_{2}
, first we argue as for
I_{1}
, splitting in
K
and
K^{c}
, to deduce that

I_{2}\leq C\int_{Z}\left(\left(\big|D\tilde{f}_{1}^{\prime}\big|\mathbf{1}_{K}\right)*\varphi_{\varepsilon}\right)^{2}+C\varepsilon^{-1}(E+\mathbf{A}^{2})^{3/2-\beta_{1}}.

(9.25)

Then, regarding the first summand in (
9.25
), we note that

|Z|\delta^{2nQ+2}\leq\int_{B_{r_{1}}\cap D}\left|\frac{\tilde{f}_{1}^{\prime}}{\sqrt{E+\mathbf{A}^{2}}}*\varphi_{\varepsilon}-\frac{\tilde{f}_{1}^{\prime}}{\sqrt{E+\mathbf{A}^{2}}}\right|^{2}\leq C\varepsilon^{2}.

(9.26)

Next, we recall that
q_{1}=2p_{1}>2
and use (
9.8
) to obtain

\displaystyle\int_{Z}\left(\left(\big|D\tilde{f}_{1}^{\prime}\big|\mathbf{1}_{K}\right)*\varphi_{\varepsilon}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq|Z|^{\frac{p_{1}-1}{p_{1}}}\left\|\left(\big|D\tilde{f}_{1}^{\prime}\big|\mathbf{1}_{K}\right)*\varphi_{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{4}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{\delta^{nQ+1}}\right)^{\frac{2\left(p_{1}-1\right)}{p_{1}}}\left\|\big|D\tilde{f}_{1}^{\prime}\big|\right\|_{L^{q_{1}}(K)}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{\delta^{nQ+1}}\right)^{\frac{2\left(p_{1}-1\right)}{p_{1}}}\left(E+\mathbf{A}^{2}\right).

(9.27)

Gathering all the estimates together (
9.20
), (
9.24
), (
9.25
) and (
9.1
) gives

\displaystyle\int_{B_{r_{1}}\cap D}\left|D\tilde{g}_{\delta}^{\prime}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{B_{r_{1}}\cap K}\big|D\tilde{f}_{1}^{\prime}\big|^{2}+C(E+\mathbf{A}^{2})\delta^{8^{-nQ-1}}+C\frac{(E+\mathbf{A}^{2})^{3/2-\beta_{1}}}{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle\quad+C(E+\mathbf{A}^{2})\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{\delta^{nQ+1}}\right)^{\frac{2\left(p_{1}-1\right)}{p_{1}}}

\displaystyle=\int_{B_{r_{1}}\cap K}\left|Df\right|^{2}-Q\int_{B_{r_{1}}\cap K}\left|D({\bm{\eta}}\circ f)\right|^{2}+C(E+\mathbf{A}^{2})\delta^{8^{-nQ-1}}

\displaystyle\quad+C\frac{(E+\mathbf{A}^{2})^{3/2-\beta_{1}}}{\varepsilon}+C(E+\mathbf{A}^{2})\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{\delta^{nQ+1}}\right)^{\frac{2\left(p_{1}-1\right)}{p_{1}}}

(9.28)

Define
Z:=\left\{\operatorname{dist}\left(({\bm{\eta}}\circ f)*\varphi_{\varepsilon},\mathcal{Q}\right)>\delta^{nQ+1}\right\}
to get

\displaystyle\int_{B_{r_{1}}\cap D}\left|D({\bm{\eta}}\circ f)*\varphi_{\varepsilon}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{B_{r_{1}}\cap D\setminus Z}\left|D\left(({\bm{\eta}}\circ f)*\varphi_{\varepsilon}\right)\right|^{2}+\int_{Z}\left|D\left(({\bm{\eta}}\circ f)*\varphi_{\varepsilon}\right)\right|^{2}

(9.29)

\displaystyle=:\hat{I}_{1}+\hat{I}_{2}.

We consider
\hat{I}_{1}
and
\hat{I}_{2}
separately. For
\hat{I}_{1}
we first observe the elementary inequality

\displaystyle\left\|D\left(({\bm{\eta}}\circ f)*\varphi_{\varepsilon}\right)\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|(D({\bm{\eta}}\circ f))*\varphi_{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\left(\left|D({\bm{\eta}}\circ f)\right|\mathbf{1}_{K}\right)*\varphi_{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\left(\left|D({\bm{\eta}}\circ f)\right|\mathbf{1}_{K^{c}}\right)*\varphi_{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+2\left\|\left(\left|D({\bm{\eta}}\circ f)\right|\mathbf{1}_{K}\right)*\varphi_{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}}\left\|\left(\left|D({\bm{\eta}}\circ f)\right|\mathbf{1}_{K^{c}}\right)*\varphi_{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}}\,.

(9.30)

Recalling
r_{1}+\varepsilon\leq r_{1}+s=r_{2}
, we estimate the first summand in (
9.30
) as follows

\left\|\left(\left|D({\bm{\eta}}\circ f)\right|\mathbf{1}_{K}\right)*\varphi_{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}\left(B_{r_{1}}\cap D\right)}^{2}\leq\int_{B_{r_{1}+\varepsilon}\cap D}\left(\left|D({\bm{\eta}}\circ f)\right|\mathbf{1}_{K}\right)^{2}\leq\int_{B_{r_{2}}\cap K}\left|D({\bm{\eta}}\circ f)\right|^{2}.

(9.31)

In order to treat the other terms, recall that
\operatorname{Lip}\left({\bm{\eta}}\circ f\right)\leq C(E+\mathbf{A}^{2})^{\beta_{1}}
and
\left|K^{c}\right|\leq C(E+\mathbf{A}^{2})^{1-2\beta_{1}}
. We thus have

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\left|D({\bm{\eta}}\circ f)\right|\mathbf{1}_{K^{c}}\right)*\varphi_{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}\left(B_{r_{1}}\cap D\right)}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(E+\mathbf{A}^{2})^{2\beta_{1}}\left\|\mathbf{1}_{K^{c}}*\varphi_{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(E+\mathbf{A}^{2})^{2\beta_{1}}\left\|\mathbf{1}_{K^{c}}\right\|_{L^{1}}^{2}\left\|\varphi_{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C(E+\mathbf{A}^{2})^{2-2\beta_{1}}}{\varepsilon}.

(9.32)

Putting (
9.31
) and (
9.32
) in (
9.30
), and recalling
E^{1-2\beta_{1}}\leq\varepsilon^{2}
and
\int\left|D({\bm{\eta}}\circ f)\right|^{2}\leq CE
we get

\hat{I}_{1}\leq\int_{B_{r_{2}}\cap D\cap K}\left|D({\bm{\eta}}\circ f)\right|^{2}+C\varepsilon^{-1}(E+\mathbf{A}^{2})^{3/2-\beta_{1}}.

(9.33)

For what concerns
\hat{I}_{2}
, first we argue as for
\hat{I}_{1}
(splitting in
K
and
K^{c}
) to deduce that

\hat{I}_{2}\leq C\int_{Z}\left(\left(\left|D({\bm{\eta}}\circ f)\right|\mathbf{1}_{K}\right)*\varphi_{\varepsilon}\right)^{2}+C\varepsilon^{-1}(E+\mathbf{A}^{2})^{3/2-\beta_{1}}.

(9.34)

Then, regarding the first summand in (
9.34
), we note that

|Z|\delta^{2nQ+2}\leq\int_{B_{r_{1}}\cap D}\left|\frac{({\bm{\eta}}\circ f)}{\sqrt{E+\mathbf{A}^{2}}}*\varphi_{\varepsilon}-\frac{({\bm{\eta}}\circ f)}{\sqrt{E+\mathbf{A}^{2}}}\right|^{2}\leq C\varepsilon^{2}.

(9.35)

Recalling that
q_{1}=2p_{1}>2
, we use (
9.8
) to obtain

\displaystyle\int_{Z}\left(\left(\left|D({\bm{\eta}}\circ f)\right|\mathbf{1}_{K}\right)*\varphi_{\varepsilon}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq|Z|^{\frac{p_{1}-1}{p_{1}}}\left\|\left(\left|D({\bm{\eta}}\circ f)\right|\mathbf{1}_{K}\right)*\varphi_{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{4}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{\delta^{\bar{n}}Q+1}\right)^{\frac{2\left(p_{1}-1\right)}{p_{1}}}\left\|\left|D({\bm{\eta}}\circ f)\right|\right\|_{L^{q_{1}}(K)}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{\delta nQ+1}\right)^{\frac{2\left(p_{1}-1\right)}{p_{1}}}\left(E+\mathbf{A}^{2}\right).

(9.36)

Gathering all the estimates together, (
9.29
), (
9.33
), (
9.34
) and (
9.36
) gives

\displaystyle\int_{B_{r_{1}}\cap D}\left|D({\bm{\eta}}\circ f)*\varphi_{\varepsilon}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{B_{r_{1}}\cap K}\left|D({\bm{\eta}}\circ f)\right|^{2}+C\frac{(E+\mathbf{A}^{2})^{3/2-\beta_{1}}}{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle\quad+C(E+\mathbf{A}^{2})\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{\delta^{nQ+1}}\right)^{2-\frac{1}{p_{1}}}.

(9.37)

So combining (
9.28
) and (
9.1
) yields

\displaystyle\int_{B_{r_{1}}\cap D}

\displaystyle\left|Dh_{\delta,\varepsilon,s}\right|^{2}=\int_{B_{r_{1}}\cap D}\left|D\hat{h}_{\delta,\varepsilon,s}\right|^{2}+Q\int_{B_{r_{1}}\cap D}\left|D\tilde{h}_{\varepsilon}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{B_{r_{1}}\cap D}\left|D\tilde{g}^{\prime}_{\delta}\right|^{2}+Q\int_{B_{r_{1}}\cap D}\left|D\tilde{h}_{\varepsilon}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{B_{r_{1}}\cap K}\left|Df\right|^{2}-Q\int_{B_{r_{1}}\cap K}\left|D({\bm{\eta}}\circ f)\right|^{2}+Q\int_{B_{r_{1}}\cap K}\left|D{\bm{\eta}}\circ f\right|^{2}+C\varepsilon(E+\mathbf{A}^{2})

\displaystyle\quad+C\left((E+\mathbf{A}^{2})\delta^{8^{-nQ-1}}+\frac{(E+\mathbf{A}^{2})^{3/2-\beta_{1}}}{\varepsilon}+(E+\mathbf{A}^{2})\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{\delta^{nQ+1}}\right)^{\frac{2\left(p_{1}-1\right)}{p_{1}}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{B_{r_{1}}\cap K}\left|Df\right|^{2}+C(E+\mathbf{A}^{2})\delta^{8^{-nQ-1}}+C\frac{(E+\mathbf{A}^{2})^{3/2-\beta_{1}}}{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle\quad+C(E+\mathbf{A}^{2})\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{\delta^{nQ+1}}\right)^{\frac{2\left(p_{1}-1\right)}{p_{1}}}+C\varepsilon(E+\mathbf{A}^{2}).

(9.38)

Step 4. Final estimate
. This part is analogue to
[
11
, Step 4 of Proposition 7.3]
. Summing (
9.18
), (
9.19
), (
9.38
), and recalling that
\varepsilon<s
, we conclude

\displaystyle\int_{B_{r_{3}}\cap D}|Dh_{\delta,\varepsilon,s}|^{2}\leq

\displaystyle\int_{B_{r_{1}}\cap K}|Df|^{2}+C\int_{(B_{r_{1}+3s}\setminus B_{r_{1}-s})\cap D}\left|Df^{\prime}\right|^{2}+C(E+\mathbf{A}^{2})\left(\varepsilon+\delta^{8^{-nQ-1}}\right)

\displaystyle+C(E+\mathbf{A}^{2})\left(\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{s^{2}}+\frac{\delta^{2\cdot 8^{-nQ}}}{s}+\frac{(E+\mathbf{A}^{2})^{1/2-\beta_{1}}}{\varepsilon}+\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{\delta^{nQ+1}}\right)^{\frac{2\left(p_{1}-1\right)}{p_{1}}}\right).

We set
\varepsilon=(E+\mathbf{A}^{2})^{a},\delta=(E+\mathbf{A}^{2})^{b}
and
s=(E+\mathbf{A}^{2})^{c},
where

a=\frac{1-2\beta_{1}}{4},\quad b=\frac{1-2\beta_{1}}{8(nQ+1)},\quad\text{ and }\quad c=\frac{1-2\beta_{1}}{8^{n}Q8(nQ+1)}

(9.39)

and we finally let
h
be the corresponding function
h_{\delta,\varepsilon,s}
.
This choice respects (
9.11
). Assume
(E+\mathbf{A}^{2})
is small enough so that
s\leq\frac{1}{16}
. Now, if
C>0
is a sufficiently large constant, there is a set
B^{\prime}\subset\left[\frac{9}{8},\frac{29}{16}\right]
with
\left|B^{\prime}\right|>1/2
such that,

\int_{(B_{r_{1}+3s}\setminus B_{r_{1}-s})\cap D}\left|Df^{\prime}\right|^{2}\leq Cs\int_{B_{2}\cap D}\left|Df^{\prime}\right|^{2}\leq C(E+\mathbf{A}^{2})^{1+c}\quad\text{ for every }r_{1}\in B^{\prime}.

For
