Blow-up analysis and conclusion

In this section we complete the proof of Theorem
4.6
, which in turn completes the proof of Theorem
0.6
. We recall the
I_{0}
from Corollary
15.6
.
The main point is the following conclusion.

Theorem 18.1
.

Let
T
be as in Assumption
15.2
and assume that
0
is not a regular point. Then
I_{0}=1
and for every
\varsigma>0

\lim_{r\downarrow 0}\frac{D(r)}{r^{2+\varsigma}}=\infty\,.

(18.1)

The latter is in contradiction with the estimate (
15.12
) (i.e.
D(r)\leq Cr^{2+\tau}
) for some positive constant
\tau
which depends on the exponent
\alpha
of Theorem
4.7
.

18.1.
Blow-up analysis

As already mentioned, Theorem
18.1
is reached through a suitable “blow-up” analysis. First of all, having fixed a sequence of
s_{j}\downarrow 0
we define a suitable family of rescalings of the maps
N_{k}^{\prime}s
. First of all we choose any
k(j)
with the property that

t_{k(j)+1}<s_{j}\leq t_{k(j)}\,.

(18.2)

Next we define the exponential map
{\mathbf{ex}}_{k}:T_{0}{\mathcal{M}}_{k}\to{\mathcal{M}}_{k}
and we identify each tangent
T_{0}{\mathcal{M}}_{k}
to
\mathbb{R}^{2}
through a suitable rotation of the ambient Euclidean space which maps it onto
\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\{0\}
.
We then consider the rescaled maps

\tilde{N}_{j}(x):=\frac{N_{k(j)}({\mathbf{ex}}_{k(j)}(s_{j}x))}{D(s_{j})^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\,.

(18.3)

The main conclusion of our blow-up analysis is the following

Theorem 18.2
.

Let
T
be as in Assumption
15.2
and assume that
0
is not a regular point. Let
s_{j}\downarrow 0
be an arbitrary vanishing sequence of positive radii, let
k(j)
be an arbitrary choice of integers satisfying (
18.2
) and let
\tilde{N}_{j}:B^{+}_{1}\to{\mathcal{A}}_{Q}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})
, where
B^{+}_{1}=B_{1}\cap\{(x_{1},x_{2}):x_{2}\geq 0\}
. Then a subsequence, not relabeled, converges strongly in
W^{1,2}(B_{1}^{+})
to a map
\tilde{N}_{\infty}
satisfying the following conditions:

(i)

\tilde{N}_{\infty}(x_{1},0)=Q\left\llbracket{0}\right\rrbracket
for all
x_{1}
;

(ii)

\tilde{N}_{\infty}
is Dir-minimizing;

(iii)

\tilde{N}_{\infty}
is
I_{0}
-homogeneous, where
I_{0}
is the positive number in Corollary
15.6
.

(iv)

{\bm{\eta}}\circ\tilde{N}_{\infty}\equiv 0
;

(v)

\int_{B_{1}^{+}}|D\tilde{N}_{\infty}|^{2}=1
.

In particular
I_{0}=1
.

Then the arguments of Theorem
5.9
apply to
\tilde{N}_{\infty}
and in particular give that
I_{0}=1
.

Proof of Theorem
18.2
.

Observe first that, following the computations of
[
8
, Section 10.1]
we conclude that

\displaystyle e^{-Cs_{j}}8^{1+I_{0}}\leq\frac{H(4s_{j})}{H(s_{j}/2)}\leq e^{Cs_{j}}8^{1+4I_{0}}

as long as
s_{j}\leq t_{k(j)}
.
Since
I_{0}
exists and is finite, there is a constant
C
(depending only on
I_{0}
) such that

D(4s_{j})\leq CD(s_{j}/2)\,.

On the other hand, arguing as in the proof of Proposition
17.1
, we easily see that

D(t_{k(j)})\geq C^{-1}t_{k(j)}^{2}{\mathbf{E}}(T,24t_{k(j)})

(we just need to choose the constant
M_{0}
appropriately large to compensate for the larger radius in the right hand side) while
D(4t_{k(j)})\leq Ct_{k(j)}^{2}{\mathbf{E}}(T,24t_{k(j)})
. Now, since the geodesic ball
\mathcal{B}_{t_{k(j)}}
in
{\mathcal{M}}_{k(j)}
contains
\{d<t_{k(j)}/2\}
while the geodesic ball
\mathcal{B}_{2t_{k(j)}}\subset\{d<4t_{k(j)}\}
, using the fact that the rescaling of the manifolds converge smoothly to the flat plane
V_{0}
, we easily conclude that

\int_{B_{2}^{+}}|D\tilde{N}_{j}|^{2}\leq C\int_{B_{1}^{+}}|D\tilde{N}_{j}|^{2}\,.

We can then follow the argument of
[
8
, Section 10.3]
to conclude that, up to subsequences,
\tilde{N}_{j}
converges strongly in the
W^{1,2}(B_{1}^{+})
topology to a Dir-minimizing map
\tilde{N}_{\infty}
. Likewise we can follow the argument of
[
8
, Section 10.2]
to conclude that
{\bm{\eta}}\circ\tilde{N}_{\infty}
vanishes identically. Recall that the maps
N_{k(j)}
vanish identically on
\Gamma
, while the rescalings of the latter converge smoothly to
T_{0}\Gamma=\{x_{2}=0\}
. The strong convergence then implies that
\tilde{N}_{\infty}=Q\left\llbracket{0}\right\rrbracket
on
\{x_{2}=0\}\cap B_{1}
. We have thus proved (i), (ii), (iv), and (v). We can however also see that

\frac{r\int\phi(r^{-1}|x|)|D\tilde{N}_{\infty}(x)|^{2}\,dx}{-\int\phi^{\prime}(r^{-1}|x|)|x|^{-1}|\tilde{N}_{\infty}(x)|^{2}\,dx}=\lim_{j\to\infty}\frac{rs_{j}D(rs_{j})}{H(rs_{j})}=I_{0}\,,

which means that the frequency function of
\tilde{N}_{\infty}
is constant. This however happens if and only if
\tilde{N}_{\infty}
is
I_{0}
-homogeneous.

As for the final statement, we invoke Theorem
5.3
.
∎

Now that we know that
I_{0}=1
, we can then conclude that by the strong convergence of
\{\tilde{N}_{j}\}_{j}
in
W^{1,2}(B_{1}^{+})
, we have

Corollary 18.3
.

If
T
is as in Theorem
18.2
, then

\lim_{r\downarrow 0}\frac{D(2r)}{D(r)}=4\,.

18.2.
Proof of (
18.1
) and conclusion

Fix
\varsigma>0
and consider the sequence of radii
r_{k}:=2^{-k}
. We know from Corollary
18.3
that, for
k
sufficiently large

D(r_{k})\geq 2^{-2-\varsigma/2}D(r_{k-1})\,.

In particular we conclude the existence of a
k_{0}
such that for every
k\geq k_{0}
, we have

D(2^{-k})\geq 2^{-(2+\varsigma/2)(k-k_{0})}D(2^{-k_{0}})\,.

In particular for every
r\leq 2^{-k_{0}}
we can write

D(r)\geq\frac{D(2^{-k_{0}})}{2^{2+\varsigma/2}}r^{2+\varsigma/2}

and since
D(2^{-k_{0}})>0
, (
18.1
) readily follows.
